We are interested in the asymptotics for the lift operator
\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}:H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})\to H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})
defined by (
27
).
Naturally, the leading-order term is provided the operator
\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}:H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})\to H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3}),
which does not depend on
\chi
. Note that for
\boldsymbol{g}\in H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})
, the function
\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{g}\in H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})
satisfies the identity

\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\left[\sym\nabla\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{g}\right]:\overline{\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{v}}=0\qquad\forall{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{v}\in H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3}),\ \boldsymbol{v}|_{\Gamma}=0.}

(41)

The operator
\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}
satisfies the estimate provided by the following lemma.

Lemma 4.1
.

There exist constants
C_{1}
,
C_{2}>0
such that for every
\boldsymbol{g}\in H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})
one has

C_{1}\left\lVert\sym\nabla\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{g}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y;\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})}\leq\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}-\frac{1}{|\Gamma|}\int_{\Gamma}\boldsymbol{g}\right\rVert_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C_{2}\left\lVert\sym\nabla\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{g}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y;\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})}.

Proof.

The first inequality is (
129
). To prove the second one we observe

\displaystyle\left\lVert\sym\nabla\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{g}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\sym\nabla\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\left(\boldsymbol{g}-\frac{1}{|\Gamma|}\int_{\Gamma}\boldsymbol{g}\right)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})}\leq\left\lVert\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\left(\boldsymbol{g}-\frac{1}{|\Gamma|}\int_{\Gamma}\boldsymbol{g}\right)\right\rVert_{H^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Estimate \eqref{piestimate2}}}}{{\leq}}C\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}-\frac{1}{|\Gamma|}\int_{\Gamma}\boldsymbol{g}\right\rVert_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}.

∎

Remark 4.2
.

Notice here that the operator
\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}
lifts the constant functions on the boundary
\boldsymbol{g}\equiv C\in\mathbb{C}^{3}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}
the to constant functions defined by the same constant:

\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{g}=\boldsymbol{G},

\boldsymbol{G}\equiv C\in\mathbb{C}^{3}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}^{\rm stiff}.

∎

The following theorem is crucial for understanding the asymptotics of the Dirichlet-to-Neumann maps. Since its proof follows a standard asymptotic argument, we provide it in Appendix.

Theorem 4.3
.

For each
n\in{\mathbb{N}},
the operator
\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}
admits the asymptotic expansion

\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}=\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}+\widetilde{\Pi}_{\chi,1}+\widetilde{\Pi}_{\chi,2}+\dots+\widetilde{\Pi}_{\chi,n}+\widetilde{\Pi}_{\chi,n}^{\rm error},\quad n\in\mathbb{N},

where the operators

\widetilde{\Pi}_{\chi,k},\widetilde{\Pi}_{\chi,n}^{\rm error}:H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})\to H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})
,
k=1,\dots,n
are bounded,
and satisfy

\left\lVert\widetilde{\Pi}_{\chi,k}\right\rVert_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})\to H^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C|\chi|^{k},\quad k=1,\dots,n,\quad\left\lVert\widetilde{\Pi}_{\chi,n}^{\rm error}\right\rVert_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})\to H^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C|\chi|^{n+1},

(42)

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, where the constant
C>0
does not depend on
\chi\in Y^{\prime}
. Furthermore, the following is valid:

\widetilde{\Pi}_{\chi,k}=\Pi_{k}:\chi^{\otimes k},\quad\left[\Pi_{k}\right]_{i_{1},i_{2},...,i_{k}}\in\mathfrak{B}\bigl(H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3}),H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})\bigr),

where the operator-valued tensors
\Pi_{k}
are symmetric:

\left[\Pi_{k}\right]_{\sigma(i_{1},i_{2},...,i_{k})}=\left[\Pi_{k}\right]_{i_{1},i_{2},...,i_{k}}\qquad\forall\sigma\in S_{n},

where
S_{n}
is the permutation group of order
n.

Remark 4.4
.

Here we make an observation that proves to be crucial in the understanding of the homogenisation properties of the effective operator (see Lemma
4.9
). By following the proof of Theorem
4.3
and observing the equation (
141
), which actually serves as the definition of the operator
\widetilde{\Pi}_{\chi,1}
, one concludes that for
\boldsymbol{g}\equiv C\in\mathbb{C}^{3}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}
one has

\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\left(\sym\nabla\widetilde{\Pi}_{\chi,1}\boldsymbol{g}+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{g}\right):\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{v}\,dy=0\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\forall\boldsymbol{v}\in H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3}),\ \boldsymbol{v}|_{\Gamma}=0.}

(43)

∎

The following lemma yields the estimates on the stiff components which are useful in the spectral analysis in the continuation of the paper. We identify the spaces of constant functions on
Y_{\rm stiff}
and
\Gamma
with the space
\mathbb{C}^{3}
.

Proposition 4.5
.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that, for all
\chi\in Y^{\prime}\setminus\{0\}
we have:

•

For every
\boldsymbol{g}\in H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})
we have:

\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}\right\rVert_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C|\chi|^{-1}\left\lVert\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{g}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})},

(44)

•

For every
\boldsymbol{g}\in H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3}),

\boldsymbol{g}\perp\mathbb{C}^{3}
(in
L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})
inner product), we have:

\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}\right\rVert_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C\left\lVert\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{g}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})}.

(45)

Proof.

The estimate (
44
) is a clear consequence of estimates (
123
), (
124
) and the trace theorem.
In order to prove (
45
), it is enough to show that for
\boldsymbol{g}\in H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})
,
\boldsymbol{g}\perp\mathbb{C}^{3}
we have:

\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C\left\lVert\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{g}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})}.

(46)

To see this, assume that (
46
) is true. By employing the trace theorem and Korn’s inequality (
122
) we calculate:

\displaystyle\lVert\boldsymbol{g}\rVert_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C\lVert\Pi^{\rm stiff}_{\chi}\boldsymbol{g}\rVert_{H^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\lVert\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{g}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})}+C\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\lVert\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{g}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})}.

Furthermore, (
46
) is an easy consequence of (
129
) which can be seen by plugging
\boldsymbol{u}=\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{g}
into (
129
). ∎

4.2
The smallest Steklov eigenvalues

The operator
\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}
on
L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})
has discrete spectrum, due to the compactness of its resolvent established above.
The eigenvalues
\nu^{\chi}
of
\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}
are equivalently characterised as solutions to either one of the following two problems:

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{g}=\nu^{\chi}\boldsymbol{g},\,\boldsymbol{g}\neq 0,\quad\boldsymbol{g}\in\mathcal{D}(\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}),

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{A}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{u}=0,\\[3.99994pt]
\Gamma_{1,\chi}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{u}=\nu^{\chi}\Gamma_{0,\chi}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{u},\quad\boldsymbol{u}\neq 0,\quad\boldsymbol{u}\in\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\mathcal{D}(\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}).\end{array}\right.

Next, we define the Rayleigh quotient associated with
\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}
, namely

\mathcal{R}_{\chi}(\boldsymbol{g}):=\frac{\lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}(\boldsymbol{g},\boldsymbol{g})}{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}^{2}},\quad\boldsymbol{g}\in H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3}),

where
\lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}
is defined by (
36
). The eigenvalues
(\nu_{n}^{\chi})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of
\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}
can be characterised with the min-max principle

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\nu_{k}^{\chi}=\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{G}\subset H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})\\
\dim\mathcal{G}=k\end{subarray}}\max_{\boldsymbol{g}\in\mathcal{G}}\mathcal{R}_{\chi}(\boldsymbol{g}),\quad k\in\mathbb{N}.

(47)

The following lemma is crucial for estimating the smallest eigenvalues.

Lemma 4.6
.

There exist constants
C_{1}>C_{2}>0
such that

•

\mathcal{R}_{\chi}(\boldsymbol{g})\geq C_{2}|\chi|^{2}\qquad\forall\boldsymbol{g}\in H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3}),

•

\mathcal{R}_{\chi}(\boldsymbol{g})\leq C_{1}|\chi|^{2}\qquad\forall\boldsymbol{g}\in\mathbb{C}^{3},

•

\mathcal{R}_{\chi}(\boldsymbol{g})\geq C_{2}\qquad\forall\boldsymbol{g}\in(\mathbb{C}^{3})^{\perp}.

Proof.

The proof of the first and third points is a direct consequence of Proposition
4.5
. The second point is verified by a direct computation. ∎

The asymptotics of eigenvalues with respect to
|\chi|
is given in the following lemma.

Lemma 4.7
.

The three smallest eigenvalues of
\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}
are of order
\mathcal{O}(|\chi|^{2}),
and the remaining eigenvalues uniformly separated from zero. Namely, there exist constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
that do not depend on
\chi
such that

c_{1}|\chi|^{2}\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\nu_{k}^{\chi}\leq c_{2}|\chi|^{2},\quad k=1,2,3,\qquad c_{1}\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\nu_{k}^{\chi},\quad k\geq 4.

We refer to
\nu_{k}^{\chi},

k=1,2,3,

as order
\mathcal{O}(|\chi|^{2})
eigenvalues and to
\nu_{k}^{\chi},

k\geq 4,
as order
\mathcal{O}(1)
eigenvalues.

Proof.

The proof is a direct consequence of (
47
) and Lemma
4.7
.
∎

Consider the decomposition

\mathcal{E}:=\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}\oplus\widecheck{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}=\widehat{P}_{\chi}\mathcal{E}\oplus\widecheck{P}_{\chi}\mathcal{E},

(48)

where
\widehat{P}_{\chi}
is the orthogonal projection onto the
three-dimensional space
\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}<\mathcal{E}
spanned by the eigenfunctions associated with order
\mathcal{O}(|\chi|^{2})
eigenvalues of
\Lambda^{\rm stiff}_{\chi},
and
\widecheck{P}_{\chi}
is the orthogonal projection onto
\widecheck{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}<\mathcal{E}
, the infinite-dimensional space spanned by the eigenfunctions associated with order
\mathcal{O}(1)
eigenvalues of
\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}
, so that

\widehat{P}_{\chi}=I-\widecheck{P}_{\chi}.

Since the decomposition (
48
) is reducing for
\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}
, we have

\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}=\begin{bmatrix}\widehat{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}&0\\[3.00003pt]
0&\widecheck{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\end{bmatrix},

where

\widehat{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}:=\widehat{P}_{\chi}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}|_{\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}},\quad\widecheck{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}:=\widecheck{P}_{\chi}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}|_{\widecheck{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}}.

(49)

Both
\widehat{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}
and
\widecheck{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}
are self-adjoint operators on
\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}
and
\widecheck{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi},
respectively (for the first one it is obvious, while for the second one it can be easily deduced). The operator
\widehat{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}
is clearly bounded since it is essentially a matrix.
Notice also that the domain of the second operator is
H^{1}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})
. Due to the Lemma
4.7
we have the uniform bound

\lVert\widehat{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\rVert_{\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}}\leq C|\chi|^{2}.

On the other hand, Lemma
4.7
implies that the operator
\widecheck{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}
, while unbounded, is uniformly bounded from below, where the estimate does not depend on
|\chi|
, namely

\lVert\bigl(\widecheck{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\bigr)^{-1}\rVert_{\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}}\leq C.

Moreover
(\widecheck{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff})^{-1}
is compact and

\bigl\lVert\bigl(\widecheck{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\bigr)^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}\bigr\rVert_{H^{1}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C\|\boldsymbol{g}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\qquad\forall\boldsymbol{g}\in L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3}),

(50)

where the constant
C
is independent of
\chi.

4.3
Asymptotics for the operator
\bigl(|\chi|^{-2}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}-I\bigr)^{-1}

The results of this Section are needed in Section
6
and Section
7
for the proof of Theorem
2.3
. They are not needed for the proof of Theorem
2.2
.
The main tool for proving Theorem
2.3
is the approximation of the Dirichlet-to-Neumann map and finding the approximating homogenized operator and its variational definition (see (
53
)).
Let us notice that, in order to do that, we cannot follow the approach of
[
10
]
, since one cannot do the expansion of eigenfunctions and eigenvalues with respect to (vector valued) quasimomentum
\chi
for systems, see
[
22
, Example 5.12]
. Instead we do the asymptotics for the resolvent of the Dirichlet-to-Neumann map and show in Section
6
and
7
that this is enough to prove Theorem
2.3
. We also notice that through the variational definition of the approximating homogenized operator (
53
) we are able to prove its characterization through
\mathbb{A}_{\rm macro}
see Lemma
4.9
.

We calculate the estimates on the distance between the resolvents of
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|\chi|^{-2}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|\chi|^{-2}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm hom}
, where the latter plays the rôle of the effective Dirichlet-to-Neumann map and is defined in the continuation of the text. In Corollary
4.13
we use this to obtain the approximation error with respect to the resolvents of
\varepsilon^{2}
scaled operators. The similar approach (in different context) was used in
[
15
,
16
]
.

Recall that the lift operator
\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}:H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})\to H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})
admits the decomposition

\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}=\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}+\widetilde{\Pi}_{\chi,1}+\widetilde{\Pi}_{\chi,1}^{\rm error},

in the sense of Theorem
4.3
, where the operator
\widetilde{\Pi}_{\chi,1}
is defined by (
141
) and its dependence on the parameter
\chi
is linear. The error term
\widetilde{\Pi}_{\chi,1}^{\rm error}
satisfies the bound (see (
42
))

\lVert\widetilde{\Pi}_{\chi,1}^{\rm error}\rVert_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})\to H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C|\chi|^{2}.

We define the effective operator to be a constant matrix
\Lambda^{\rm hom}_{\chi}\in\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3}
such that

\displaystyle\left\langle-\Lambda^{\rm hom}_{\chi}\boldsymbol{c},\boldsymbol{d}\right\rangle=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\bigl[\sym\nabla\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{c}_{\rm corr}+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{c}+\sym\nabla\widetilde{\Pi}_{\chi,1}\boldsymbol{c}\bigr]:\overline{\bigl[{\rm i}X_{\chi}\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{d}+\sym\nabla\widetilde{\Pi}_{\chi,1}\boldsymbol{d}\bigr]}

(51)

\displaystyle\forall\boldsymbol{c},\boldsymbol{d}\in\mathbb{C}^{3},

where
\boldsymbol{c}_{\rm corr}\in H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})
is the unique solution with
\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}c_{\rm corr}=0
to the cell problem

\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\left[\sym\nabla\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{c}_{\rm corr}+\Xi_{\chi}\boldsymbol{c}\right]:\overline{\sym\nabla\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{v}}=0\qquad\forall\boldsymbol{v}\in H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3}).

(52)

where we denote
\Xi_{\chi}:={\rm i}X_{\chi}\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}+\sym\nabla\widetilde{\Pi}_{\chi,1}
.
The definition of homogenised operator takes the form

\left\langle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Lambda^{\rm hom}_{\chi}\boldsymbol{c},\boldsymbol{d}\right\rangle:=\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\left[\sym\nabla\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{c}_{\rm corr}+\Xi_{\chi}\boldsymbol{c}\right]:\overline{\Xi_{\chi}\boldsymbol{d}}.

(53)

In next two lemmas we give important properties of the operator (matrix)
\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm hom}
.

Lemma 4.8
.

There exists a constant
\nu>0
such that

\nu|\chi|^{2}|\boldsymbol{c}|^{2}\leq\langle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm hom}\boldsymbol{c},\boldsymbol{c}\rangle\leq\nu^{-1}|\chi|^{2}|\boldsymbol{c}|^{2}
for all
\boldsymbol{c}\in\mathbb{C}^{3}.

Proof.

Our first observation is the formula

\left\langle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Lambda^{\rm hom}_{\chi}\boldsymbol{c},\boldsymbol{d}\right\rangle=\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\left[\sym\nabla\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{c}_{\rm corr}+\Xi_{\chi}\boldsymbol{c}\right]:\overline{\left[\sym\nabla\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{d}_{\rm corr}+\Xi_{\chi}\boldsymbol{d}\right]},

(54)

where
\boldsymbol{d}_{\rm corr}
is defined by (
52
) from
\boldsymbol{d}
,
so
\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm hom}
is a Hermitian matrix. Also, we have the coercivity bound

\displaystyle\left\langle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Lambda^{\rm hom}_{\chi}\boldsymbol{c},\boldsymbol{c}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\left[\sym\nabla\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{c}_{\rm corr}+\Xi_{\chi}\boldsymbol{c}\right]:\overline{\left[\sym\nabla\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{c}_{\rm corr}+\Xi_{\chi}\boldsymbol{c}\right]}

\displaystyle\geq C\left\lVert\sym\nabla\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{c}_{\rm corr}+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{c}+\sym\nabla\widetilde{\Pi}_{\chi,1}\boldsymbol{c}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})}^{2}

\displaystyle=C\left(\left\lVert\sym\nabla\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{c}_{\rm corr}+\sym\nabla\widetilde{\Pi}_{\chi,1}\boldsymbol{c}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})}^{2}+\left\lVert{\rm i}X_{\chi}\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{c}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq C|\chi|^{2}\left\lVert\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{c}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}^{2}\geq C|\chi|^{2}|\boldsymbol{c}|^{2}.

∎

Lemma 4.9
.

The matrix
\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm hom}
is quadratic in
\chi,
namely

\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm hom}=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|\Gamma|^{-1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left({\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)^{*}\mathbb{A}_{\rm macro}{\rm i}X_{\chi},

where
\mathbb{A}_{\rm macro}

is the constant symmetric tensor defined by (
2
).

Proof.

The first step is to conclude that for
\boldsymbol{c}\in\mathbb{C}^{3}
, the equation (
52
) can actually be tested against a wider class of functions, namely

\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\left[\sym\nabla\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{c}_{\rm corr}+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{c}+\sym\nabla\widetilde{\Pi}_{\chi,1}\boldsymbol{c}\right]:\overline{\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{v}}=0\qquad\forall\boldsymbol{v}\in H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3}).

(55)

This can be seen by noting that for an arbitrary
\boldsymbol{v}\in H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})
one has the decomposition

\boldsymbol{v}=\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{h}+\boldsymbol{w},\qquad\boldsymbol{h}\in H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3}),\qquad\boldsymbol{w}\in H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3}),\quad\boldsymbol{w}|_{\Gamma}=0.

The vanishing of the expression (
55
) when testing against functions of the type
\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{h}
,
\boldsymbol{h}\in H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})
is stated in (
52
), while the case of testing with
\boldsymbol{w}\in H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3}),

\boldsymbol{w}=0\mbox{ on }\Gamma
is covered with (
41
) and (
43
). Introducing the notation
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{u}_{\rm corr}^{j}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}:=\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{c}_{\rm corr}^{j}+\widetilde{\Pi}_{\chi,1}\boldsymbol{c}^{j}
,
j=1,2,
where
\boldsymbol{c}^{1},\boldsymbol{c}^{2}\in\mathbb{C}^{3}
,
and invoking (
51
) we have

\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\bigl[\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{\rm corr}^{j}+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{c}^{j}\bigr]:\overline{\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{v}}=0\quad\forall\boldsymbol{v}\in H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3}),\quad j=1,2,

and also

\displaystyle\left\langle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Lambda^{\rm hom}_{\chi}\boldsymbol{c}^{1},\boldsymbol{c}^{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle:=\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\left[\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{\rm corr}^{1}+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{c}^{1}\right]:\overline{\left[\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{\rm corr}^{2}+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{c}^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle=\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\left[\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{\rm corr}^{1}+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{c}^{1}\right]:\overline{\left[\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{\rm corr}^{2}+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{c}^{2}\right]}=\mathbb{A}_{\rm macro}{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{c}^{1}:\overline{{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{c}^{2}},

where we have employed the definition of the macroscopic tensor (
2
).
∎

Next, we state the theorem on the norm-resolvent estimates.

Theorem 4.10
.

There exists a constant
C>0
, which does not depend on
\chi
, such that the following norm-resolvent estimate holds:

\left\lVert\left(|\chi|^{-2}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}-I\right)^{-1}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(|\chi|^{-2}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm hom}-I\right)^{-1}S\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})\to H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C|\chi|.

Here
S:\boldsymbol{h}\mapsto|\Gamma|^{-1}\int_{\Gamma}\boldsymbol{h}
is the averaging operator on
\Gamma,

and
\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm hom}
is the effective operator defined by (
51
).

Proof.

The proof of this theorem is constructive in nature.
We start from the weak formulation of the resolvent problem for the operator
\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}
. For
\boldsymbol{h}\in L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3}),
our goal is to expand the solution
\boldsymbol{g}
of the problem

\frac{1}{|\chi|^{2}}\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{g}:\overline{\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{v}}+\int_{\Gamma}\boldsymbol{g}\cdot\overline{\boldsymbol{v}}=\int_{\Gamma}\boldsymbol{h}\cdot\overline{\boldsymbol{v}}\qquad\forall\boldsymbol{v}\in H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})

(56)

in the form

\boldsymbol{g}=\boldsymbol{g}_{0}+\boldsymbol{g}_{1}+\boldsymbol{g}_{2}+\boldsymbol{g}_{\rm err},

where the terms satisfy the bounds

\boldsymbol{g}_{0}=\mathcal{O}(1),\quad\boldsymbol{g}_{1}=\mathcal{O}(|\chi|),\quad\boldsymbol{g}_{2}=\mathcal{O}(|\chi|^{2}),\quad\boldsymbol{g}_{\rm err}=\mathcal{O}(|\chi|^{3}),

with respect to the
H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})
norm, and are calculated from a sequence of boundary value problems obtained from (
56
), which are introduced in the continuation of the proof.
By equating the terms in (
56
) which are of order
\mathcal{O}(1)
, we obtain the following identity for the leading-order term
\boldsymbol{g}_{0}:

\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\sym\nabla\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{g}_{0}:\overline{\sym\nabla\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{v}}=0\qquad\forall\boldsymbol{v}\in H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3}).

From here, it is clear that the term
\boldsymbol{g}_{0}=\boldsymbol{c}\in\mathbb{C}^{3}
. However, at this point, we cannot determine
\boldsymbol{g}_{0}
yet.
Furthermore, by combining the terms of order
\mathcal{O}(|\chi|)
, we obtain the identity

\begin{split}\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\sym\nabla\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{g}_{1}:\overline{\sym\nabla\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{v}}=-\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\Xi_{\chi}\boldsymbol{g}_{0}:\overline{\sym\nabla\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{v}}\qquad\forall\boldsymbol{v}\in H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3}),\end{split}

(57)

which has a unique solution satisfying
\int_{\Gamma}\boldsymbol{g}_{1}=0
. Furthermore, comparing the terms of order
\mathcal{O}(|\chi|^{2})
, we define
\boldsymbol{g}_{2}
as the solution to the identity

\begin{split}\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\sym\nabla\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{g}_{2}&:\overline{\sym\nabla\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{v}}=\\[3.50006pt]
&-\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}(\Xi_{\chi}\boldsymbol{g}_{1}+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\rm i}X_{\chi}\widetilde{\Pi}_{\chi,1}\boldsymbol{g}_{0}+\sym\nabla\widetilde{\Pi}_{\chi,1}^{\rm error}\boldsymbol{g}_{0})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}:\overline{\sym\nabla\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{v}}\\[3.50006pt]
&-\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\sym\nabla\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{g}_{1}+\Xi_{\chi}\boldsymbol{g}_{0})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}:\overline{\Xi_{\chi}\boldsymbol{v}}\\[3.50006pt]
&-|\chi|^{2}\int_{\Gamma}\boldsymbol{g}_{0}\cdot\overline{\boldsymbol{v}}+|\chi|^{2}\int_{\Gamma}\boldsymbol{h}\cdot\overline{\boldsymbol{v}}\qquad\forall\boldsymbol{v}\in H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3}).\end{split}

(58)

The uniqueness and existence of solution to this equation can be established with two additional constraints. We require
\boldsymbol{g}_{2}
to have zero mean:
\int_{\Gamma}\boldsymbol{g}_{2}=0
, and that the right-hand side vanishes when tested against constants
1
1

1

This is in fact a Fredholm alternative
.
There is a priori no reason for the second constraint to be valid. However, we still have an additional degree of freedom in the choice of
\boldsymbol{g}_{0}\in\mathbb{C}^{3},
which can be used here to obtain well posedness of the problem. Setting
\boldsymbol{v}=\boldsymbol{v}_{0}\in\mathbb{C}^{3}
in (
58
) yields

\begin{split}0=&-\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}(\sym\nabla\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{g}_{1}+\Xi_{\chi}\boldsymbol{g}_{0}):\overline{\Xi_{\chi}\boldsymbol{v}_{0}}-|\chi|^{2}\int_{\Gamma}\boldsymbol{g}_{0}\cdot\overline{\boldsymbol{v}_{0}}+|\chi|^{2}\int_{\Gamma}\boldsymbol{h}\cdot\overline{\boldsymbol{v}_{0}}\qquad\forall\boldsymbol{v}_{0}\in\mathbb{C}^{3}.\end{split}

(59)

Using (
54
) and (
57
), it follows from (
59
) that

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{1}{|\chi|^{2}}\left\langle\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm hom}\boldsymbol{g}_{0},\boldsymbol{v}_{0}\right\rangle+\int_{\Gamma}\boldsymbol{g}_{0}\cdot\overline{\boldsymbol{v}_{0}}=\int_{\Gamma}\boldsymbol{h}\cdot\overline{\boldsymbol{v}_{0}}\qquad\forall\boldsymbol{v}_{0}\in\mathbb{C}^{3},

or, equivalently,

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{1}{|\chi|^{2}}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm hom}\boldsymbol{g}_{0}+\boldsymbol{g}_{0}=\frac{1}{|\Gamma|}\int_{\Gamma}\boldsymbol{h}.

(60)

Thus, by defining the leading-order term as the solution to the above resolvent problem, we have ensured the solvability of (
58
).

Next, we prove the estimates for the correctors, and the final error estimate.
It is clear from (
60
) and the coercivity of
\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm hom}
that

\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}_{0}\right\rVert_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C\left\lVert\boldsymbol{h}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}.

Furthermore, from (
57
) we have

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\lVert\sym\nabla\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y;\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})}\leq\left\lVert{\rm i}X_{\chi}\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{g}_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y;\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})}+\bigl\lVert\sym\nabla\widetilde{\Pi}_{\chi,1}\boldsymbol{g}_{0}\bigr\rVert_{L^{2}(Y;\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

By virtue of Theorem
4.3
and Proposition
8.12
, we obtain

\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\right\rVert_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C|\chi|\left\lVert\boldsymbol{h}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}.

(61)

In a similar manner, it can be seen from (
58
) that

\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}_{2}\right\rVert_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C|\chi|^{2}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{h}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}.

(62)

Next, we formulate the equation for the error term
\boldsymbol{g}_{\rm err}:=\boldsymbol{g}-\boldsymbol{g}_{0}-\boldsymbol{g}_{1}-\boldsymbol{g}_{2}
, where

\boldsymbol{g}
is the solution to the full problem (
56
).

\frac{1}{|\chi|^{2}}\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{g}_{\rm err}\cdot\overline{\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{v}}=\frac{1}{|\chi|^{2}}\mathcal{R}_{\rm err}(\boldsymbol{v})\quad\forall\boldsymbol{v}\in H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3}),

(63)

where the functional
\mathcal{R}_{\rm err}
satisfies the bound

\bigl|\mathcal{R}_{\rm err}(\boldsymbol{v})\bigr|\leq C|\chi|^{3}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{v}\right\rVert_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{h}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}.

Thus, by testing (
63
) with
\boldsymbol{g}_{\rm err}
and invoking (
44
), we obtain

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}_{\rm err}\right\rVert_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}^{2}\leq\frac{C}{|\chi|^{2}}\left\lVert\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{g}_{\rm err}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y;\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})}^{2}\leq\frac{C}{|\chi|^{2}}\bigl|\mathcal{R}_{\rm err}(\boldsymbol{g}_{\rm err})\bigr|\leq C|\chi|\lVert\boldsymbol{g}_{\rm err}\rVert_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{h}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

In other words,

\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}-\boldsymbol{g}_{0}-\boldsymbol{g}_{1}-\boldsymbol{g}_{2}\right\rVert_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C|\chi|\left\lVert\boldsymbol{h}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}.

From (
61
) and (
62
), it follows that
\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}-\boldsymbol{g}_{0}\right\rVert_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C|\chi|\left\lVert\boldsymbol{h}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})},

as required. ∎

Remark 4.11
.

The averaging operator
S:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}
coincides with the projection operator
\widehat{P}_{0}=\widehat{P}_{\chi}|_{\chi=0}
.
∎

Remark 4.12
.

The norm-resolvent estimate provided in Theorem
4.10
also yields the estimate

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\lVert\left(|\chi|^{-2}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}-zI\right)^{-1}-\left(|\chi|^{-2}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm hom}-zI\right)^{-1}S\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})\to H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C(z)|\chi|,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

where
C(z)
depends on the distance of
z
to the spectrum of
|\chi|^{-2}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(hom)}
and
|z|
(It is bounded when
\left({\rm dist}\left(z,\sigma\left(|\chi|^{-2}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(hom)}\right)\right)\right)^{-1}
and
|z|
are bounded). This can be seen by using resolvent identities or by exploring the proof of Theorem
4.10
.
∎

The following is an interesting stand-alone result regarding the resolvent asymptotics of the Dirichlet-to-Neumann maps on the stiff component.

Corollary 4.13
.

There exists a constant
C>0
, independent of
\chi\in Y^{\prime}
,
\varepsilon>0
, such that the following norm-resolvent estimate holds:

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\varepsilon^{-2}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}-I\right)^{-1}-\left(\varepsilon^{-2}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm hom}-I\right)^{-1}S\right\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})\to H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C\varepsilon,

where the homogenised operator
\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm hom}
is defined by the formula (
51
).

Before proceeding to the proof, we first provide some important auxiliary results.
There are several crucial remarks which allow us to rewrite the norm resolvent estimates in terms of the parameter
\varepsilon
.
Both operators
|\chi|^{-2}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}
and
|\chi|^{-2}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm hom}
(where the latter is in fact a multiplication by a constant matrix depending on
\chi
) have exactly
3
eigenvalues of order
\mathcal{O}(1)
, and the set of these eigenvalues can be enclosed with a fixed contour
\gamma\subset\mathbb{C}
uniformly in
|\chi|
(for small
|\chi|
).

Lemma 4.14
.

There exists constants
\zeta,\eta>0
and a contour
\gamma\subset\mathbb{C}
(
\Re(z)<\eta
, for every
z\in\gamma
) such that, for all
0<|\chi|\leq\zeta:

•

The contour
\gamma
encloses all eigenvalues of order
\mathcal{O}(1)
of both operators
|\chi|^{-2}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}
and
|\chi|^{-2}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm hom};

•

The contour
\gamma
does not enclose any other eigenvalue of
|\chi|^{-2}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}
and
|\chi|^{-2}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm hom}
;

•

There exists
\rho_{0}>0
,
\inf_{z\in\gamma}|z-\nu^{\chi}|\geq\rho_{0}
, where
\nu^{\chi}
is an eigenvalue of

|\chi|^{-2}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}

(|\chi|^{-2}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm hom})
.

Proof.

The proof of this fact stems from the fact that the spectrum of order
\mathcal{O}(1)
is uniformly separated from the rest for small enough
|\chi|
. This is guaranteed by the estimates in Lemma
4.7
. The same estimates also provide the fact that this part of the spectrum lies in a fixed interval independent of
\chi
.
∎

For every fixed
\varepsilon>0
,
\chi\neq 0
we define the function

g_{\varepsilon,\chi}(z):=\left(\frac{|\chi|^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}z-1\right)^{-1},\;\Re(z)<0,

which satisfies the following lemma:

Lemma 4.15
.

For every fixed
\eta>0
, function
g_{\varepsilon,\chi}
is bounded on the half-plane
\{z\in\mathbb{C},\Re(z)\leq-\eta\}
,

|g_{\varepsilon,\chi}(z)|\leq C\left(\max\left\{\frac{|\chi|^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}},1\right\}\right)^{-1}.

Proof.

\displaystyle\bigl|g_{\varepsilon,\chi}(z)\bigr|^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\biggl|\frac{|\chi|^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}z\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0}1\biggr|\geq\dfrac{|\chi|^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\eta+1\geq\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\dfrac{|\chi|^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}&\mbox{if $|\chi|^{2}\gg\varepsilon^{2}$},\\[10.00002pt]
1&\mbox{if $\varepsilon^{2}\approx|\chi|^{2}$}.\end{array}\right.

∎

Proof of Corollary
4.13
.

Applying the Cauchy formula, we obtain

\begin{split}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widehat{P}_{\chi}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}-I\right)^{-1}\widehat{P}_{\chi}=\frac{1}{2\pi{\rm i}}\oint_{\gamma}g_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z)\left(zI-\frac{1}{|\chi|^{2}}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\right)^{-1}dz,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\\
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\Lambda^{\rm hom}_{\chi}-I\right)^{-1}S=\frac{1}{2\pi{\rm i}}\oint_{\gamma}g_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z)\left(zI-\frac{1}{|\chi|^{2}}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm hom}\right)^{-1}Sdz,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\end{split}

where
\widehat{P}_{\chi}
is the operator of projection onto the
3
-dimensional span of the eigenfunctions of
\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}
associated with eigenvalues of order
|\chi|^{2}.
Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\right)^{-1}-\widehat{P}_{\chi}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\right)^{-1}\widehat{P}_{\chi}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})\to H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}

(65)

\displaystyle=\left\|(I-\widehat{P}_{\chi})\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\right)^{-1}(I-\widehat{P}_{\chi})\right\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})\to H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C\varepsilon^{2},

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{P}_{\chi}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\right)^{-1}\widehat{P}_{\chi}-\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm hom}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\right)^{-1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}S\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})\to H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}

(66)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2\pi}\oint_{\gamma}|g_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z)|\left\|\left(zI-\frac{1}{|\chi|^{2}}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\right)^{-1}-\left(zI-\frac{1}{|\chi|^{2}}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm hom}\right)^{-1}S\right\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})\to H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}dz

\displaystyle\leq C|\chi|\left(\max\left\{\frac{|\chi|^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}},1\right\}\right)^{-1}\leq C\varepsilon,

where we have used Remark
4.12
.
The proof follows by combining (
65
) and (
66
). ∎

Theorem
4.10
has another direct consequence, namely the asymptotics of the spectral projections
\widehat{P}_{\chi}
and the truncated lift operators
\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\widehat{P}_{\chi}
with respect to the quasimomentum
\chi
.

Corollary 4.16
.

The operators
\widehat{P}_{\chi}
and
\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\widehat{P}_{\chi}
satisfy the asymptotics

\displaystyle\left\lVert\widehat{P}_{\chi}-\widehat{P}_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})\to H^{1}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C|\chi|,

(67)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\widehat{P}_{\chi}-\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\widehat{P}_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})\to H^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C|\chi|.

(68)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\widehat{P}_{\chi}\left(\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\right)^{*}-\widehat{P}_{0}\left(\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\right)^{*}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})\to L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C|\chi|.

(69)

where the constant
C>0
does not depend on
\chi\in Y^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By choosing a contour
\gamma
as above, and applying the Cauchy formula to the constant function
g(z)=1
, we obtain the asymptotics of projectors
\widehat{P}_{\chi}
defined by (
48
):

\displaystyle\widehat{P}_{\chi}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\gamma}\left(zI-\frac{1}{|\chi|^{2}}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\right)^{-1}dz

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\gamma}\left(zI-\frac{1}{|\chi|^{2}}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm hom}\right)^{-1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}S\color[rgb]{0,0,0}dz+\oint_{\gamma}\widetilde{R}_{\chi}^{\rm corr}(z)dz

\displaystyle=\widehat{P}_{0}+\oint_{\gamma}\widetilde{R}_{\chi}^{\rm corr}(z)dz,

where

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{R}_{\chi}^{\rm corr}(z):=\left(|\chi|^{-2}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}-zI\right)^{-1}-\left(|\chi|^{-2}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm hom}-zI\right)^{-1}S,\quad\biggl\lVert\oint_{\gamma}\widetilde{R}_{\chi}^{\rm corr}(z)dz\biggr\rVert_{L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})\to H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C|\chi|,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

as a consequence of Remark
4.12
. This proves the estimate

\left\lVert\widehat{P}_{\chi}-\widehat{P}_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})\to H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C|\chi|.

(70)

To improve it, we write it in the following way:

\widehat{P}_{\chi}-\widehat{P}_{0}=\widehat{P}_{\chi}(\widehat{P}_{\chi}-\widehat{P}_{0})+(\widehat{P}_{\chi}-\widehat{P}_{0})\widehat{P}_{0}.

(71)

Notice that also
\widehat{P}_{\chi}:L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})\to H^{1}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})
is bounded as a consequence of the fact that
\mathcal{D}\left(\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\right)=H^{1}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})
and that
\widehat{P}_{\chi}
is the projection onto the eigenspace corresponding to eigenvalues of order
|\chi|^{2}
. The first term in (
71
) is bounded in
L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})\to H^{1}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})
norm with order
|\chi|
due to (
70
) and the boundedness of
\widehat{P}_{\chi}
. The second term in (
71
) is also bounded in the same norm with the same order, as a consequence of (
70
) and the fact that
\widehat{P}_{0}
is a projection onto a fixed finite-dimensional subspace of
H^{1}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})
. Combining this with the result of Theorem
4.3
, it follows that

\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\widehat{P}_{\chi}=\left(\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}+\widetilde{\Pi}_{\chi,1}^{\rm error}\right)\left(\widehat{P}_{0}+\widetilde{P}_{\chi}^{\rm error}\right)=\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\widehat{P}_{0}+\mathcal{O}(|\chi|),

where
O(|\chi|)
is understood in the sense of the
L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})\to H^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})
operator norm. Similarly,

\widehat{P}_{\chi}\left(\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\right)^{*}=\widehat{P}_{0}\left(\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\right)^{*}+\mathcal{O}(|\chi|),

where
O(|\chi|)
is understood in the sense of the
L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})\to L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})
operator norm.
∎

4.4
Asymptotics on the soft component

Next, we state some asymptotic properties of the boundary operators for the soft component.

Lemma 4.17
.

For the boundary operators on the soft component, one has

\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm soft}={\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\chi y}\Pi_{0}^{\rm soft}{\rm e}^{{\rm i}\chi y},\qquad\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm soft}={\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\chi y}\Lambda_{0}^{\rm soft}{\rm e}^{{\rm i}\chi y},\qquad\Gamma_{\chi,1}^{\rm soft}={\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\chi y}\Gamma_{0,1}^{\rm soft}{\rm e}^{{\rm i}\chi y}.

(72)

Moreover, the eigenvalues of the operator
\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi}^{\rm soft}
are independent of
\chi,
and

{\rm e}^{{\rm i}\chi y}\bigl(\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi}^{\rm soft}-zI\bigr)^{-1}{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\chi y}=\bigl(\mathcal{A}_{0,0}^{\rm soft}-zI\bigr)^{-1},

(73)

where the operator
\mathcal{A}_{0,0}^{\rm soft}
is defined by (
26
) (with
\chi=0
).
Furthermore, let
(\mu_{n})_{n}
be a sequence of eigenvalues of the operator
\mathcal{A}_{0,0}^{\rm soft}
and
(\varphi_{n})_{n}
the associated sequence of eigenfunctions. Then the corresponding sequence of eigenfunctions of
\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi}^{\rm soft}
is given by
(\varphi_{n}^{\chi})_{n}=({\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\chi y}\varphi_{n})_{n}
.

Proof.

The proof is based on the fact that
H^{1}_{\chi}(Y_{\rm soft};\mathbb{C}^{3})=H^{1}(Y_{\rm soft};\mathbb{C}^{3}).
Recall the definition of
\Pi_{0}^{\rm soft}
:

\Pi_{0}^{\rm soft}\boldsymbol{g}=\boldsymbol{u}\quad\iff\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}-\div\left(\mathbb{A}_{\rm soft}\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\right)=0&\mbox{ on }Y_{\rm soft},\\[2.5pt]
\boldsymbol{u}=\boldsymbol{g}&\mbox{ on }\Gamma.\end{array}\right.

Writing
\boldsymbol{u}={\rm e}^{{\rm i}\chi y}\boldsymbol{w}\in H^{1}(Y_{\rm soft};\mathbb{C}^{3})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Pi_{0}^{\rm soft}\boldsymbol{g}=\boldsymbol{u}

\displaystyle\iff\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-\div\left(\mathbb{A}_{\rm soft}\sym\nabla\left({\rm e}^{{\rm i}\chi y}\boldsymbol{w}\right)\right)=0&\mbox{ on }Y_{\rm soft},\\[2.5pt]
{\rm e}^{{\rm i}\chi y}\boldsymbol{w}=\boldsymbol{g}&\mbox{ on }\Gamma\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\iff\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-\div\left({\rm e}^{{\rm i}\chi y}\mathbb{A}_{\rm soft}\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\boldsymbol{w}\right)=0&\mbox{ on }Y_{\rm soft},\\[2.5pt]
\boldsymbol{w}={\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\chi y}\boldsymbol{g}&\mbox{ on }\Gamma\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\iff\boldsymbol{w}=\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm soft}{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\chi y}\boldsymbol{g}\quad\iff\quad\boldsymbol{u}={\rm e}^{{\rm i}\chi y}\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm soft}{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\chi y}\boldsymbol{g}.

Similarly, one has

\displaystyle\Lambda_{0}^{\rm soft}\boldsymbol{g}=\boldsymbol{h}

\displaystyle\iff\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}-\div\left(\mathbb{A}_{\rm soft}\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\right)=0&\mbox{ on }Y_{\rm soft},\\[2.5pt]
\boldsymbol{u}=\boldsymbol{g}&\mbox{ on }\Gamma,\\[2.5pt]
\mathbb{A}_{\rm soft}\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\cdot n=\boldsymbol{h}&\mbox{ on }\Gamma\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\iff\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}-\div\left(\mathbb{A}_{\rm soft}\sym\nabla\left({\rm e}^{{\rm i}\chi y}\boldsymbol{w}\right)\right)=0&\mbox{ on }Y_{\rm soft},\\[2.5pt]
{\rm e}^{{\rm i}\chi y}\boldsymbol{w}=\boldsymbol{g}&\mbox{ on }\Gamma,\\[2.5pt]
\mathbb{A}_{\rm soft}\sym\nabla\left({\rm e}^{{\rm i}\chi y}\boldsymbol{w}\right)\cdot n=\boldsymbol{h}&\mbox{ on }\Gamma\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\iff\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}-\div\left({\rm e}^{{\rm i}\chi y}\mathbb{A}_{\rm soft}\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\boldsymbol{w}\right)=0&\mbox{ on }Y_{\rm soft},\\[2.5pt]
\boldsymbol{w}={\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\chi y}\boldsymbol{g}&\mbox{ on }\Gamma,\\[2.5pt]
\mathbb{A}_{\rm soft}\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\boldsymbol{w}\cdot n={\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\chi y}\boldsymbol{h}&\mbox{ on }\Gamma\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\iff{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\chi y}\boldsymbol{h}=\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm soft}{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\chi y}\boldsymbol{g}\quad\iff\quad\boldsymbol{h}={\rm e}^{{\rm i}\chi y}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm soft}{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\chi y}\boldsymbol{g}.

∎

Corollary 4.18
.

For the
M
-function
M_{\chi}^{\rm soft}(z)
of the soft component, one has

M_{\chi}^{\rm soft}(z)={\rm e}^{-i\chi y}M_{0}^{\rm soft}(z){\rm e}^{i\chi y},\qquad M_{0}^{\rm soft}(z)=\Lambda_{0}^{\rm soft}+z\left(\Pi_{0}^{\rm soft}\right)^{*}\Pi_{0}^{\rm soft}+z^{2}\left(\Pi_{0}^{\rm soft}\right)^{*}\left(\mathcal{A}_{0,0}^{\rm soft}-zI\right)^{-1}\Pi_{0}^{\rm soft}.

Furthermore, there exists a
\chi
-independent constant
C=C(z)>0
such that

\left\lVert\widehat{P}_{\chi}M_{\chi}^{\rm soft}(z)\widehat{P}_{\chi}-\widehat{P}_{0}M_{0}^{\rm soft}(z)\widehat{P}_{0}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}}\leq C|\chi|.

Proof.

By recalling the identity (
20
), we have the representation formula

M_{\chi}^{\rm soft}(z)=\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm soft}+z\left(\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm soft}\right)^{*}\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm soft}+z^{2}\left(\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm soft}\right)^{*}\bigl(\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi}^{\rm soft}-zI\bigr)^{-1}\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm soft}.

Employing the identities (
72
) and (
73
), as well as Remark
3.15
and (
67
) yields the claim.
∎

Remark 4.19
.

Notice that, due to the fact that
\Lambda_{0}^{\rm soft}|_{\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{0}}=0
, one also has

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M_{0}^{\rm soft}(z)|_{\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{0}}=z\left(\Pi_{0}^{\rm soft}\right)^{*}\Pi_{0}^{\rm soft}|_{\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{0}}+z^{2}\left(\Pi_{0}^{\rm soft}\right)^{*}\left(\mathcal{A}_{0,0}^{\rm soft}-zI\right)^{-1}\Pi_{0}^{\rm soft}|_{\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{0}}

(74)

∎

The following corollary is easy to prove.

Corollary 4.20
.

The operator
\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm soft}\widehat{P}_{\chi}
satisfies the asymptotics

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm soft}\widehat{P}_{\chi}-\Pi_{0}^{\rm soft}\widehat{P}_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})\to L^{2}(Y_{\rm soft};\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C|\chi|,

(75)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\widehat{P}_{\chi}\left(\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm soft}\right)^{*}-\widehat{P}_{0}\left(\Pi_{0}^{\rm soft}\right)^{*}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm soft};\mathbb{C}^{3})\to L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C|\chi|.

where the constant
C>0
does not depend on
\chi\in Y^{\prime}
.

Proof.

The proof is the direct consequence of (
72
) and (
67
).
∎

5
\varepsilon^{2}
-order resolvent asymptotics of the transmission boundary problem

In this section we aim at proving Theorem
2.2
. The starting point is the Krein formula (
40
). The approximation is carried out in two steps. The first step (see Section
5.1
, Theorem
5.2
) is to use the Schur-Frobenius inversion formula by restricting the traces onto the space
\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}
, as well as imposing the equality of projections to the same space of the traces of co-normal derivatives. The second step
(Section
5.2
, Theorem
5.5
) is to approximate the
M
-function. We recall the formulae (
39
), (
38
) and (
40
).

Recalling (
15
), we write

S_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}(\varepsilon^{2}z)=\Bigl(I+\varepsilon^{2}z\bigl(\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi}^{\rm stiff}-\varepsilon^{2}zI\bigr)^{-1}\Bigr)\Pi^{\rm stiff}_{\chi}=\Pi^{\rm stiff}_{\chi}+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{2}),

(76)

where
\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{2})
is understood in the sense of the
\mathcal{E}\mapsto\mathcal{H}^{\rm stiff}
norm. The formula (
20
) yields

\displaystyle M_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}(\varepsilon^{2}z)

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}+\varepsilon^{2}z\left(\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\right)^{*}\left(I-\varepsilon^{2}z\left(\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi}^{\rm stiff}\right)^{-1}\right)^{-1}\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}

(77)

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}+\varepsilon^{2}z\left(\left(\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\right)^{*}\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\right)+\varepsilon^{4}z^{2}\left(\left(\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\right)^{*}\left(\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi}^{\rm stiff}-\varepsilon^{2}zI\right)^{-1}\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\right).

We can also obtain the full expansion by using the following geometric series (in the
L^{2}\to L^{2}
sense):

\Bigl(I-\varepsilon^{2}z\bigl(\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi}^{\rm stiff}\bigr)^{-1}\Bigr)^{-1}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\varepsilon^{2n}z^{n}\bigl(\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi}^{\rm stiff}\bigr)^{-n}.

This yields

\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}M_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}(\varepsilon^{2}z)=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}+z\left(\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\right)^{*}\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\varepsilon^{2n}z^{n}\left(\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\right)^{*}\bigl(\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi}^{\rm stiff}\bigr)^{-n}\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}.

5.1
Steklov truncation

Similarly as in (
49
), we define the following truncated Dirichlet-to-Neumann maps:

\widehat{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm soft}:=\widehat{P}_{\chi}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm soft}|_{\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}},\quad\widecheck{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm soft}:=\widecheck{P}_{\chi}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm soft}|_{\widecheck{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}},\qquad\widehat{\Lambda}_{\chi,\varepsilon}:=\widehat{P}_{\chi}\Lambda_{\chi,\varepsilon}|_{\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}},\quad\widecheck{\Lambda}_{\chi,\varepsilon}:=\widecheck{P}_{\chi}\Lambda_{\chi,\varepsilon}|_{\widecheck{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}},

so one obviously has

\widehat{\Lambda}_{\chi,\varepsilon}=\varepsilon^{-2}\widehat{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}+\widehat{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm soft},\quad\widecheck{\Lambda}_{\chi,\varepsilon}=\varepsilon^{-2}\widecheck{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}+\widecheck{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm soft}.

The first operator sum is well defined due to the fact that these objects are essentially Hermitian matrices. The second sum defines a self-adjoint operator, by the similar argument as in the Theorem
3.17
, where the domain is given by

\mathcal{D}(\widecheck{\Lambda}_{\chi,\varepsilon})=\mathcal{D}(\widecheck{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff})=\mathcal{D}(\widecheck{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm soft})=\widecheck{P}_{\chi}\bigl(\mathcal{D}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\Lambda_{\chi,\varepsilon})\bigr).\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Additionally, we denote the truncated lift operators by

\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}:=\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}|_{\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}},\quad\widecheck{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}:=\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}|_{\widecheck{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}},\quad\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}:=\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\oplus\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm soft},\quad\widecheck{\Pi}_{\chi}:=\widecheck{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\oplus\widecheck{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm soft}.

Thus, we have defined the following “Steklov-truncated” Ryzhov triples

\bigl(\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)},\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)},\widehat{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}\bigr)\mbox{ on }\bigl(\mathcal{H}^{\rm stiff(soft)},\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}\bigr),\quad\bigl(\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)},\widecheck{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)},\widecheck{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}\bigr)\mbox{ on }\bigl(\mathcal{H}^{\rm stiff(soft)},\widecheck{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}\bigr),

as well as the coupled Steklov-truncated triples

(\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi,\varepsilon},\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi},\widehat{\Lambda}_{\chi,\varepsilon}),

(\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi,\varepsilon},\widecheck{\Pi}_{\chi},\widecheck{\Lambda}_{\chi,\varepsilon})
on
(\mathcal{H},\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}),

(\mathcal{H},\widecheck{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}),
respectively.
The triple properties as stated in the Definition
3.1
are easily checked. For example, one has

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi,\varepsilon})\cap\mathcal{R}(\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi,\varepsilon})\cap\Pi_{\chi}(\widehat{P}_{\chi}(\mathcal{E}))\subset\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi,\varepsilon})\cap\Pi_{\chi}(\mathcal{E})=\{0\},

\displaystyle\ker(\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi})

\displaystyle=\ker(\Pi_{\chi}\widehat{P}_{\chi})\subset\ker(\Pi_{\chi})=\{0\}.

One can define the boundary triples and
M
-functions associated with the Ryzhov triples introduced above, denoted in the same fashion. Notice that, for example, the domain of the operator
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{\chi}^{\rm soft}
coincides with
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi}^{\rm soft})\dot{+}\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}(\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi})
, so the trace operator
\widehat{\Gamma}_{0,\chi}
takes values in
\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}
.
By recalling that representation formula (
17
) decomposes the
M
-function into the sum of a self-adjoint operator (Dirichlet-to-Neumann map) and a bounded operator, we know that its domain actually coincides with the domain of the associated “
\Lambda
-operator”, and thus one has

\displaystyle\widehat{M}_{\chi}(z)^{\rm stiff(soft)}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}+z\bigl(\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}\bigr)^{*}\Bigl(I-z\bigl(\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}\bigr)^{-1}\Bigr)^{-1}\widehat{\Pi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}_{\chi}

\displaystyle=\widehat{P}_{\chi}\Bigl(\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}+z\bigl(\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}\bigr)^{*}\Bigl(I-z\bigl(\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}\bigr)^{-1}\Bigr)^{-1}\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}\Bigr)\Big|_{\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}}

\displaystyle=\widehat{P}_{\chi}M_{\chi}(z)^{\rm stiff(soft)}\big|_{\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}},

and a similar claim holds for
\widecheck{M}_{\chi}(z)^{\rm stiff(soft)}
,
\widecheck{M}_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z),
and
\widehat{M}_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z)
.

We introduce the notation

\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}:=\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi},

together with the notation
P_{\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}}
for the respective orthogonal projections (with respect to the
\mathcal{H}
inner product). We also define
\Theta_{\chi}:\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}
as the orthogonal projection

\Theta_{\chi}\left({\boldsymbol{u}}^{\rm soft}\oplus{\boldsymbol{u}}^{\rm stiff}\right)={\boldsymbol{u}}^{\rm soft}\oplus P_{\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}}\boldsymbol{u}^{\rm stiff}.

(78)

Before stating our approximation result, we need one helpful lemma, whose proof is found in the Appendix. It establishes the equivalence of the
H^{1}
and
L^{2}
norms on
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}
uniformly in the quasimomentum
\chi.

Lemma 5.1
.

There exists a
\chi
-independent constant
C>0
such that

\lVert\boldsymbol{f}\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff(soft)};\mathbb{C}^{3})}\geq C\lVert\boldsymbol{f}\rVert_{H^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff(soft)};\mathbb{C}^{3})}\qquad\forall\boldsymbol{f}\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}.

The following theorem is the first approximation result.

Theorem 5.2
.

There exists
C>0

such that for the resolvent of the transmission boundary problem (
10
) one has

\Bigl\lVert\bigl((\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\varepsilon})_{0,I}-zI\bigr)^{-1}-\bigl(\left(\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\varepsilon}\right)_{\widecheck{P}_{\chi},\widehat{P}_{\chi}}-zI\bigr)^{-1}\Bigr\rVert_{\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}}\leq C\varepsilon^{2}\qquad\forall\chi\in Y^{\prime},\ z\in K_{\sigma}.

Proof.

Notice that, as a consequence of (
20
) and (
39
), we can write:

M_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z)=\Lambda_{\chi,\varepsilon}+\mathcal{B}_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z)=\varepsilon^{-2}\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}+\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm soft}+\mathcal{B}_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z),

(79)

where the operator
\mathcal{B}_{\chi,\varepsilon}
is bounded uniformly in
\chi,{\varepsilon}
. So, the question of boundedness of a certain truncation of
M_{\chi,\varepsilon}
actually comes down to the boundedness of associated truncation of Dirichlet-to-Neumann map. But, since
\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}\subset\mathcal{D}(\Lambda_{\chi,\varepsilon})=\mathcal{D}(\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm stiff})=\mathcal{D}(\Lambda_{\chi}^{\rm soft})
and
\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}
is finite-dimensional, one has

\left\lVert\widehat{P}_{\chi}\Lambda_{\chi,\varepsilon}\widecheck{P}_{\chi}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}}\leq\left\lVert\Lambda^{\rm soft}_{\chi,\varepsilon}\big|_{\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}},\qquad\left\lVert\widecheck{P}_{\chi}\Lambda_{\chi,\varepsilon}\widehat{P}_{\chi}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}}\leq\left\lVert\Lambda^{\rm soft}_{\chi,\varepsilon}\big|_{\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}},

and thus the operators
\widehat{P}_{\chi}M_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z)\widecheck{P}_{\chi}
,
\widecheck{P}_{\chi}M_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z)\widehat{P}_{\chi}
, are bounded as well, uniformly in
\chi,{\varepsilon}
. We next show that the operator
\widecheck{P}_{\chi}M_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z)\widecheck{P}_{\chi}
is boundedly invertible with a bound depending on
\varepsilon
. This is the point where we stress the importance of Steklov truncations and the bound (
50
).

To prove this, our first observation is that the operator
\widecheck{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm soft}(\widecheck{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff})^{-1}
is bounded independently of
\chi
, as a consequence of (
35
) and (
50
). Using the formula (
79
), we have

\widecheck{M}_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z)=\Bigl(I+\varepsilon^{2}\widecheck{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm soft}\bigl(\widecheck{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\bigr)^{-1}+\varepsilon^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widecheck{P}_{\chi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{B}_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z)\bigl(\widecheck{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\bigr)^{-1}\Bigr)\varepsilon^{-2}\widecheck{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}.

(80)

Since, the operators
\widecheck{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm soft}(\widecheck{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff})^{-1}
and
\mathcal{B}_{\chi,\varepsilon}(\widecheck{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff})^{-1}
are bounded uniformly in
\chi,{\varepsilon}
, we can choose
\varepsilon
small enough so that we can invert the whole (
80
) while

\Bigl\lVert\Bigl(I+\varepsilon^{2}\widecheck{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm soft}\bigl(\widecheck{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\bigr)^{-1}+\varepsilon^{2}\mathcal{B}_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z)\bigl(\widecheck{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\bigr)^{-1}\Bigr)^{-1}\Bigr\rVert_{\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

(81)

Combining (
80
) and (
81
) yields

\lVert\widecheck{M}_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z)^{-1}\rVert_{\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}}\leq C\varepsilon^{2}.

We write
M_{\chi,\varepsilon}
in a block matrix decomposition, relative to the decomposition (
48
):

M_{\chi,\varepsilon}=\begin{bmatrix}\mathbb{A}&\mathbb{B}\\
\mathbb{E}&\mathbb{F}\end{bmatrix},

where

\mathbb{A}:=\widehat{M}_{\chi,{\varepsilon}}(z),\quad\mathbb{B}:=\widehat{P}_{\chi}M_{\chi,{\varepsilon}}(z)\widecheck{P}_{\chi},\quad\mathbb{E}:=\widecheck{P}_{\chi}M_{\chi,{\varepsilon}}(z)\widehat{P}_{\chi},\quad\mathbb{F}:=\widecheck{M}_{\chi,{\varepsilon}}(z).

where we have shown that
\mathbb{A}
,
\mathbb{B}
,
\mathbb{E}
are bounded (the bound of
\mathbb{A}
depends on
{\varepsilon}
), and
\mathbb{F}
is boundedly invertible

\lVert\mathbb{F}^{-1}\rVert_{\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}}\leq C\varepsilon^{2},

where
C
does not depend on
{\varepsilon}
. Our next aim is to show that
\mathbb{A}
is boundedly invertible with the bound independent of
\chi
.
To this end, notice that (
77
) implies

{\varepsilon}^{-2}\widehat{M}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}({\varepsilon}^{2}z)={\varepsilon}^{-2}\widehat{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}+z\bigl(\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\bigr)^{*}\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}+O({\varepsilon}^{2}),

(82)

where
O({\varepsilon}^{2})
is understood in the sense of the
L^{2}\to L^{2}
operator norm, uniformly in
\chi.
Using (
19
), (
82
), Lemma
5.1
, the trace inequality, and the fact that
z\in K_{\sigma},
we infer the existence of a
\chi
-independent constant
C>0
such that, for all
\boldsymbol{f}\in L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})
and
{\varepsilon}
small enough, one has

\displaystyle\Bigl|\bigl\langle\Im\widehat{M}_{\chi,{\varepsilon}}(z)\boldsymbol{f},\boldsymbol{f}

\displaystyle\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{E}}\Bigr|=\Bigl|\bigl\langle{\varepsilon}^{-2}\Im\widehat{M}^{\rm stiff}_{\chi}({\varepsilon}^{2}z)\boldsymbol{f},\boldsymbol{f}\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{E}}\Bigr|+\Bigl|\bigl\langle\Im\widehat{M}^{\rm soft}_{\chi}(z)\boldsymbol{f},\boldsymbol{f}\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{E}}\Bigr|\geq\Bigl|\bigl\langle{\varepsilon}^{-2}\Im\widehat{M}^{\rm stiff}_{\chi}({\varepsilon}^{2}z)\boldsymbol{f},\boldsymbol{f}\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{E}}\Bigr|

\displaystyle=|\Im z|\|\widehat{\Pi}^{\rm stiff}_{\chi}\boldsymbol{f}\|_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}+O({\varepsilon}^{2})\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}\geq C\|\widehat{\Pi}^{\rm stiff}_{\chi}\boldsymbol{f}\|_{H^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}+O({\varepsilon}^{2})\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}

\displaystyle\geq C\|\widehat{P}_{\chi}\boldsymbol{f}\|_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}.

By virtue of Corollary
8.2
, it now follows that

\left\lVert\mathbb{A}^{-1}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}}\leq C,

(83)

where
C>0
does not depend on
\chi.

Using the Schur-Frobenius inversion formula, see
[
39
]
, we have

M_{\chi,\varepsilon}^{-1}(z)=\overline{\begin{bmatrix}\mathbb{A}&\mathbb{B}\\
\mathbb{E}&\mathbb{F}\end{bmatrix}}^{-1}=\begin{bmatrix}\mathbb{A}^{-1}&0\\
0&0\end{bmatrix}+\begin{bmatrix}\overline{\mathbb{A}^{-1}\mathbb{B}}\overline{\mathbb{S}}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\mathbb{A}^{-1}&-\overline{\mathbb{A}^{-1}\mathbb{B}}\overline{\mathbb{S}}^{-1}\\
\overline{\mathbb{S}}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\mathbb{A}^{-1}&\overline{\mathbb{S}}^{-1}\end{bmatrix},

where
\mathbb{S}:=\mathbb{F}-\mathbb{E}\mathbb{A}^{-1}\mathbb{B}
. However, since

\left\lVert\mathbb{S}^{-1}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}}\leq\left\lVert\left(I-\mathbb{F}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\mathbb{A}^{-1}\mathbb{B}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{F}^{-1}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}}\leq C\varepsilon^{2},

we have

M_{\chi,\varepsilon}^{-1}(z)=\begin{bmatrix}\bigl(\widehat{P}_{\chi}M_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z)\widehat{P}_{\chi}\bigr)^{-1}&0\\
0&0\end{bmatrix}+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{2})=\widehat{P}_{\chi}\bigl(\widehat{P}_{\chi}M_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z)\widehat{P}_{\chi}\bigr)^{-1}\widehat{P}_{\chi}+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{2}).

On the other hand, by Schur-Frobenius’ formula, we have

\displaystyle\bigl(\widecheck{P}_{\chi}+\widehat{P}_{\chi}M_{\varepsilon,\chi}(z)\bigr)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\widehat{P}_{\chi}M_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z)\widehat{P}_{\chi}&\widehat{P}_{\chi}M_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z)\widecheck{P}_{\chi}\\
0&I\end{bmatrix}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\bigl(\widehat{P}_{\chi}M_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z)\widehat{P}_{\chi}\bigr)^{-1}&-\bigl(\widehat{P}_{\chi}M_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z)\widehat{P}_{\chi}\bigr)^{-1}\widehat{P}_{\chi}M_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z)\widecheck{P}_{\chi}\\
0&I\end{bmatrix}.

Now, clearly

M_{\chi,\varepsilon}^{-1}(z)=\bigl(\widecheck{P}_{\chi}+\widehat{P}_{\chi}M_{\varepsilon,\chi}(z)\bigr)^{-1}\widehat{P}_{\chi}+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{2}).

Thus, by putting
\beta_{0}=\widecheck{P}_{\chi}
,
\beta_{1}=\widehat{P}_{\chi}
and recalling the Theorem
3.9
we recognise that the operator-valued function

z\mapsto\left(\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi,\varepsilon}-zI\right)^{-1}-S_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z)\left(\widecheck{P}_{\chi}+\widehat{P}_{\chi}M_{\varepsilon,\chi}(z)\right)^{-1}\widehat{P}_{\chi}S_{\chi,\varepsilon}(\overline{z})^{*}

is the resolvent of the closed extension of
\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi,\varepsilon}
associated with the boundary condition

\bigl(\widecheck{P}_{\chi}\Gamma_{0,\chi}+\widehat{P}_{\chi}\Gamma_{1,\chi}\bigr)\boldsymbol{u}=0

in the sense of Theorem
3.9
. Thus we have proven the desired result.
∎

Remark 5.3
.

The resolvent
(\left(\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\varepsilon}\right)_{\widecheck{P}_{\chi},\widehat{P}_{\chi}}-zI)^{-1}
is associated with boundary value problem (
\boldsymbol{f}\in\mathcal{H}
)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\varepsilon}\boldsymbol{u}-z\boldsymbol{u}=\boldsymbol{f},\\[5.69046pt]
\bigl(\widecheck{P}_{\chi}\Gamma_{0,\chi}+\widehat{P}_{\chi}\Gamma_{1,\chi}\bigr)\boldsymbol{u}=0,\end{array}\right.

The solution
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathcal{H}
satisfies the following constraints:

•

On one hand, only the
3
-dimensional component which lies in the space
\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}
of the traces of their co-normal derivatives (from inside
Y_{\rm soft}
,
Y_{\rm stiff}
respectively) coincide on the boundary
\Gamma
.

•

On the other hand, the traces from both inside
Y_{\rm soft}
and
Y_{\rm stiff}
actually belong to the
3
-dimensional space
\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}
(they clearly coincide, as seen in Remark
3.18
).

Thus, by approximating the resolvent
((\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\varepsilon})_{0,I}-zI)^{-1}
associated with the transmission problem (
10
) by the resolvent
((\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\varepsilon})_{\widecheck{P}_{\chi},\widehat{P}_{\chi}}-zI)^{-1}
, one relaxes the condition on the continuity of co-normal derivatives and tightens the constraint on the traces, while making an error of order
\varepsilon^{2}.

The standard intuition yields that the homogenisation procedure should replace the solution on the stiff component with the
3
-dimensional constant vector. However, in this step, the finite-dimensionality is only imposed on the trace of the solution.
∎

5.2
Refinement of the approximation: truncation of
\widehat{M}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}

We introduce the following notation for the truncated solution operator
\widehat{S}_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z):=S_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z)|_{\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}}
.
Using the formula (
16
), we have

\displaystyle\widehat{S}_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z):=S_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z)\big|_{\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}}

\displaystyle=\bigl(\Pi_{\chi}+z\bigl(\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi,\varepsilon}-zI\bigr)^{-1}\Pi_{\chi}\bigr)\big|_{\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}}=\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}+z\bigl(\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi,\varepsilon}-zI\bigr)^{-1}\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi},

(84)

which is precisely the solution operator associated with the triple
(\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi,\varepsilon},\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi},\widehat{\Lambda}_{\chi,\varepsilon})
in the sense of Definition
3.3
. Similar representation formulae are obtained for the operators
\widehat{S}^{\rm soft(stiff)}_{\chi}(z)
(with an obvious definition).

Remark 5.4
.

Notice that

\bigl(\widehat{S}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}(z)\bigr)^{*}=\widehat{P}_{\chi}\bigl(S_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}\bigr)^{*},\quad\bigl(\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}\bigr)^{*}=\widehat{P}_{\chi}\bigl(\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}\bigr)^{*}.

(85)

Also, one has

P_{\mathcal{H}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}}\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}=\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}\widehat{P}_{\chi},\quad\bigl(\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}\bigr)^{*}P_{\mathcal{H}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}}=\widehat{P}_{\chi}\bigl(\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}\bigr)^{*},

(86)

which follows directly from the definition.
∎

The operator
\widehat{\Gamma}_{0,\chi}
is left inverse to the operator
\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}
in the sense of the Definition
3.2
, so it is clear, due to (
84
), that it is the left inverse to the operator
\widehat{S}_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z)
as well. Similar holds for the operators
\widehat{S}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}(z)
. In particular, we have

\widehat{\Gamma}_{0,\chi}\widehat{S}_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z)=\widehat{\Gamma}_{0,\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}\widehat{S}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff(soft)}(z)=I|_{\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}}.

In the Theorem
5.2
we have obtained an approximation of the original resolvent in terms of the resolvent of another operator, where the relative simplification is not immediately evident. However, by doing simple additional approximations the result becomes much clearer. We perform these additional approximations by separately analyzing the block components of the resolvent (see (
85
))

\displaystyle\bigl(\left(\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\varepsilon}\right)_{\widecheck{P}_{\chi},\widehat{P}_{\chi}}-zI\bigr)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi,\varepsilon}-zI\right)^{-1}-S_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z)\widehat{P}_{\chi}\bigl(\widehat{P}_{\chi}M_{\varepsilon,\chi}(z)\widehat{P}_{\chi}\bigr)^{-1}\widehat{P}_{\chi}S_{\chi,\varepsilon}(\overline{z})^{*}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi,\varepsilon}-zI\right)^{-1}-\widehat{S}_{\chi,\varepsilon}(z){\widehat{M}_{\varepsilon,\chi}(z)}^{-1}\widehat{S}_{\chi,\varepsilon}(\overline{z})^{*},

relative to the decomposition (
24
).
It follows from (
82
) and (
83
) that

{\widehat{M}_{\varepsilon,\chi}(z)}^{-1}=\bigl(\varepsilon^{-2}\widehat{M}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}(\varepsilon^{2}z)+\widehat{M}_{\chi}^{\rm soft}(z)\bigr)^{-1}=\bigl(\varepsilon^{-2}\widehat{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}+z\bigl(\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\bigr)^{*}\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}+\widehat{M}_{\chi}^{\rm soft}(z)\bigr)^{-1}+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{2}).

(87)

We introduce the following operator-valued function which plays a great rôle in the formulation of the homogenisation results:

Definition 5.1
.

We refer to the operator valued function
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widehat{Q}^{\rm app}_{\chi,\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(z):\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}\to\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}
given with the following expression:

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widehat{Q}^{\rm app}_{\chi,\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(z):=\varepsilon^{-2}\widehat{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}+z\bigl(\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\bigr)^{*}\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}+\widehat{M}_{\chi}^{\rm soft}(z)

(88)

as the
transmission function
.

We have the following result on resolvent asymptotics in which we significantly simplify the solution on the stiff component in the approximation of the resolvent.

Theorem 5.5
.

There exists
C>0
, which depends only on
\sigma
and
{\rm diam}(K_{\sigma})
, such that for the resolvent of the transmission boundary problem (
10
) we have:

\bigl\lVert\bigl(\left(\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\varepsilon}\right)_{0,I}-zI\bigr)^{-1}-\mathcal{R}_{\chi,\varepsilon}^{\rm app}(z)\bigr\rVert_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}}\leq C\varepsilon^{2},

for all
\chi\in Y^{\prime}
, where the operator valued function
\mathcal{R}_{\chi,\varepsilon}^{\rm app}(z)
is defined by the following formula:

\mathcal{R}_{\chi,\varepsilon}^{\rm app}(z):=\begin{bmatrix}\bigl(\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi}^{\rm soft}-zI\bigr)^{-1}-\widehat{S}^{\rm soft}_{\chi}(z){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widehat{Q}^{\rm app}_{\chi,\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(z)}^{-1}\widehat{S}_{\chi}^{\rm soft}(\overline{z})^{*}&-\widehat{S}^{\rm soft}_{\chi}(z){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widehat{Q}^{\rm app}_{\chi,\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(z)}^{-1}\bigl(\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\bigr)^{*}\\[5.0pt]
-\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widehat{Q}^{\rm app}_{\chi,\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(z)}^{-1}\widehat{S}_{\chi}^{\rm soft}(\overline{z})^{*}&-\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widehat{Q}^{\rm app}_{\chi,\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(z)}^{-1}\bigl(\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\bigr)^{*}\end{bmatrix},

(89)

relative to the decomposition
\mathcal{H}^{\rm soft}\oplus\mathcal{H}^{\rm stiff}
.

Proof.

The proof consists of applying the asymptotic formula (
87
) to the separate blocks of the resolvent
(\left(\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\varepsilon}\right)_{0,I}-zI)^{-1}
. We have

\displaystyle P_{\rm soft}\bigl((\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\varepsilon})_{\widecheck{P}_{\chi},\widehat{P}_{\chi}}-zI\bigr)^{-1}P_{\rm soft}=

\displaystyle\bigl(\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi}^{\rm soft}-zI\bigr)^{-1}-\widehat{S}^{\rm soft}_{\chi}(z){\widehat{M}_{\varepsilon,\chi}(z)}^{-1}\widehat{S}_{\chi}^{\rm soft}(\overline{z})^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigl(\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi}^{\rm soft}-zI\bigr)^{-1}-\widehat{S}^{\rm soft}_{\chi}(z){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widehat{Q}^{\rm app}_{\chi,\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(z)}^{-1}\widehat{S}_{\chi}^{\rm soft}(\overline{z})^{*}+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{2}).

Next, we use the asymptotic formula (
76
) for
S_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}
:

\displaystyle P_{\rm stiff}\bigl((\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\varepsilon})_{\widecheck{P}_{\chi},\widehat{P}_{\chi}}-zI\bigr)^{-1}P_{\rm soft}=

\displaystyle-\widehat{S}^{\rm stiff}_{\chi}(\varepsilon^{2}z){\widehat{M}_{\varepsilon,\chi}(z)}^{-1}\widehat{S}_{\chi}^{\rm soft}(\overline{z})^{*}=-\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widehat{Q}^{\rm app}_{\chi,\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(z)}^{-1}\widehat{S}_{\chi}^{\rm soft}(\overline{z})^{*}+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{2}),

\displaystyle P_{\rm soft}\bigl((\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\varepsilon})_{\widecheck{P}_{\chi},\widehat{P}_{\chi}}-zI\bigr)^{-1}P_{\rm stiff}=

\displaystyle-\widehat{S}^{\rm soft}_{\chi}(z){\widehat{M}_{\varepsilon,\chi}(z)}^{-1}\widehat{S}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}(\varepsilon^{2}\overline{z})^{*}=-\widehat{S}^{\rm soft}_{\chi}(z){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widehat{Q}^{\rm app}_{\chi,\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(z)}^{-1}\bigl(\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\bigr)^{*}+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{2}).

For calculating the remaining block, we use the fact that

P_{\rm stiff}\left(\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi,\varepsilon}-zI\right)^{-1}P_{\rm stiff}=\bigl(\varepsilon^{-2}\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi}^{\rm stiff}-zI\bigr)^{-1}=\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{2})

in
\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}
operator norm. Finally, we have

\displaystyle P_{\rm stiff}\bigl((\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\varepsilon})_{\widecheck{P}_{\chi},\widehat{P}_{\chi}}-zI\bigr)^{-1}P_{\rm stiff}=

\displaystyle\bigl(\varepsilon^{-2}\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi}^{\rm stiff}-zI\bigr)^{-1}-\widehat{S}^{\rm stiff}_{\chi}(z){\widehat{M}_{\varepsilon,\chi}(z)}^{-1}\widehat{S}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}(\overline{z})^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widehat{Q}^{\rm app}_{\chi,\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(z)}^{-1}\bigl(\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\bigr)^{*}+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{2}),

which concludes the proof. ∎

Remark 5.6
.

Notice that we can rewrite (
89
) in the following way:

\mathcal{R}_{\chi,\varepsilon}^{\rm app}(z):=\bigl(\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi}^{\rm soft}-zI\bigr)^{-1}P_{\mathcal{H}^{\rm soft}}-\begin{bmatrix}\widehat{S}^{\rm soft}_{\chi}(z)&\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\end{bmatrix}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widehat{Q}^{\rm app}_{\chi,\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(z)}^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}\widehat{S}^{\rm soft}_{\chi}(z)&\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\end{bmatrix}^{*}.

(90)

∎

5.3
The fiberwise approximating operator

It remains to identify the selfadjoint operator which resolvent is in a sense represented with (
89
).
To this end, we consider the Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}^{\rm soft}\oplus\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}
and define the following operator on it:

Definition 5.2
.

\mathcal{D}\bigl(\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\varepsilon}^{\rm app}\bigr):=\bigl\{(\boldsymbol{u},\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}})\in\mathcal{H}^{\rm soft}\oplus\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff},\quad\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathcal{D}(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{\chi}^{\rm soft}),\quad\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}=\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\widehat{\Gamma}_{0,\chi}^{\rm soft}\boldsymbol{u}\bigr\},

(91)

\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\varepsilon}^{\rm app}\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{u}\\
\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}\end{bmatrix}:=\begin{bmatrix}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{\chi}^{\rm soft}&0\\
-\bigl(\bigl(\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\bigr)^{*}\bigr)^{-1}\widehat{\Gamma}_{1,\chi}^{\rm soft}&-\varepsilon^{-2}\bigl(\bigl(\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\bigr)^{*}\bigr)^{-1}\widehat{\Gamma}_{1,\chi}^{\rm stiff}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{u}\\
\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}\end{bmatrix}.

(92)

The following theorem establishes the link between the resolvent of the operator (
92
) and the operator valued function (
89
).

Theorem 5.7
.

For every
\chi\in Y^{\prime}
, the operator
\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\varepsilon}^{\rm app}
is self-adjoint and its resolvent for all
z\in\rho(\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\varepsilon}^{\rm app})
is given by the formula (
89
), relative to the decomposition
\mathcal{H}^{\rm soft}\oplus\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}.

Proof.

First we show that the operator
\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\varepsilon}^{\rm app}
is symmetric. For
(\boldsymbol{u},\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}),(\boldsymbol{v},\widehat{\boldsymbol{v}})\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\varepsilon}^{\rm app})
we calculate:

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\langle\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\varepsilon}^{\rm app}\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{u}\\
\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}\end{bmatrix},\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{v}\\
\widehat{\boldsymbol{v}}\end{bmatrix}\right\rangle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

\displaystyle=\bigl\langle\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{\chi}^{\rm soft}\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{H}^{\rm soft}}-\Bigl\langle\bigl(\bigl(\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\bigr)^{*}\bigr)^{-1}\widehat{\Gamma}_{1,\chi}^{\rm soft}\boldsymbol{u},\widehat{\boldsymbol{v}}\Bigr\rangle_{\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}}-\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\Bigl\langle\bigl(\bigl(\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\bigr)^{*}\bigr)^{-1}\widehat{\Gamma}_{1,\chi}^{\rm stiff}\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{v}}\Bigr\rangle_{\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{\chi}^{\rm soft}\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}^{\rm soft}}-\left\langle\left(\left(\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\right)^{*}\right)^{-1}\widehat{\Gamma}_{1,\chi}^{\rm soft}\boldsymbol{u},\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\widehat{\Gamma}_{0,\chi}^{\rm soft}\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle_{\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\langle\left(\left(\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\right)^{*}\right)^{-1}\widehat{\Gamma}_{1,\chi}^{\rm stiff}\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\widehat{\Gamma}_{0,\chi}^{\rm soft}\boldsymbol{u},\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\widehat{\Gamma}_{0,\chi}^{\rm soft}\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle_{\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{\chi}^{\rm soft}\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}^{\rm soft}}-\left\langle\widehat{\Gamma}_{1,\chi}^{\rm soft}\boldsymbol{u},\widehat{\Gamma}_{0,\chi}^{\rm soft}\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle_{\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}}-\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\langle\widehat{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\widehat{\Gamma}_{0,\chi}^{\rm soft}\boldsymbol{u},\widehat{\Gamma}_{0,\chi}^{\rm soft}\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle_{\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi}}.

By the Green formula (see (
13
)) and by the self-adjointness of
\widehat{\Lambda}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}
one has

\left\langle\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\varepsilon}^{\rm app}\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{u}\\
\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}\end{bmatrix},\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{v}\\
\widehat{\boldsymbol{v}}\end{bmatrix}\right\rangle=\left\langle\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{u}\\
\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}\end{bmatrix},\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\varepsilon}^{\rm app}\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{v}\\
\widehat{\boldsymbol{v}}\end{bmatrix}\right\rangle.

Next, we fix
\boldsymbol{f}\in\mathcal{H}^{\rm soft}
,
\,\;\widehat{\!\!\boldsymbol{f}}\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}
. For every
z\in\rho(\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\varepsilon}^{\rm app})
we consider the problem

\left(\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\varepsilon}^{\rm app}-zI\right)\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{u}\\
\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{f}\\
\,\;\widehat{\!\!\boldsymbol{f}}\end{bmatrix},\quad\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{u}\\
\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathcal{D}\left(\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\varepsilon}^{\rm app}\right).

Component-wise, we have

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{\chi}^{\rm soft}\boldsymbol{u}-z\boldsymbol{u}=\boldsymbol{f},\\
-\left(\left(\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\right)^{*}\right)^{-1}\widehat{\Gamma}_{1,\chi}^{\rm soft}\boldsymbol{u}-\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\left(\widehat{\Pi}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\right)^{*}\right)^{-1}\widehat{\Gamma}_{1,\chi}^{\rm stiff}\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}-z\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}=\,\;\widehat{\!\!\boldsymbol{f}},\end{array}\right.\quad\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{u}\\
\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathcal{D}\left(\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\varepsilon}^{\rm app}\right),

(93)

which is equivalent to

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{\chi}^{\rm soft}\boldsymbol{u}-z\boldsymbol{u}=\boldsymbol{f},\\
