\left\lVert{\rm e}^{{\rm i}\chi y}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{3})}=\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{3})},\qquad\left\lVert\nabla({\rm e}^{{\rm i}\chi y}\boldsymbol{u})\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{3})}=\bigl\lVert\nabla\boldsymbol{u}+\boldsymbol{u}\otimes{\rm i}\chi\bigr\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{3})}.

Now, we calculate

\bigl\lVert{\rm e}^{{\rm i}\chi y}\boldsymbol{u}\bigr\rVert_{H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{3})}^{2}=\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{3})}^{2}+\bigl\lVert\nabla\boldsymbol{u}+\boldsymbol{u}\otimes{\rm i}\chi\bigr\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{3})}^{2}\leq\left(1+|\chi|^{2}\right)\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{3})}^{2}+\left\lVert\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{3})}^{2}\leq C\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{3})}^{2}.

Conversely:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{3})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{3})}^{2}+\left\lVert\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{3})}^{2}\leq\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{3})}^{2}+\bigl\lVert\nabla\boldsymbol{u}+\boldsymbol{u}\otimes{\rm i}\chi\bigr\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{3})}^{2}+|\chi|^{2}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{3})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(1+|\chi|^{2}\right)\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{3})}^{2}+\bigl\lVert\nabla\boldsymbol{u}+\boldsymbol{u}\otimes{\rm i}\chi\bigr\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{3})}^{2}\leq C\lVert{\rm e}^{{\rm i}\chi y}\boldsymbol{u}\rVert_{H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{3})}.

∎

The following proposition easily follows.

Proposition 8.8
.

Let
\Omega
be a bounded open set with Lipschitz boundary and
B\subset\partial\Omega
,
|B|>0
. There exists a constant
C
such that for every
\boldsymbol{u}\in H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{3})
and
|\chi|\in Y^{\prime}
, we have the following estimate:

\lVert\boldsymbol{u}\rVert_{H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C\left(\left\lVert\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})}+\lVert\boldsymbol{u}\rVert_{L^{2}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B\color[rgb]{0,0,0};\mathbb{C}^{3})}\right),

(122)

where the constant
C
depends only on the domain
\Omega
.

Proof.

By plugging
\boldsymbol{w}={\rm e}^{i\chi y}\boldsymbol{u}
,
\boldsymbol{u}\in H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{3})
, in the inequality (
119
), we obtain from Proposition
8.7
:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq\left\lVert\boldsymbol{w}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\lVert\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})}+\lVert\boldsymbol{w}\rVert_{L^{2}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B\color[rgb]{0,0,0};\mathbb{C}^{3})}\right)

\displaystyle=C\left(\left\lVert\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})}+\lVert\boldsymbol{u}\rVert_{L^{2}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B\color[rgb]{0,0,0};\mathbb{C}^{3})}\right),

where we have also used (
121
).
∎

The following proposition gives us necessary estimates for periodic functions with periodicity cell
Y
.

Proposition 8.9
.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that for all
\boldsymbol{u}\in H_{\#}^{1}(Y;\mathbb{C}^{3})
,
\chi\in Y^{\prime}\setminus\{0\}
one has

\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}H^{1}(Y;\mathbb{C}^{3})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leq\frac{C}{|\chi|}\left\lVert\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y;\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})}.

(123)

\left\lVert\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y;\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})}\leq C\left\lVert\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y;\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})}.

(124)

\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}-\fint_{Y}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}H^{1}(Y;\mathbb{C}^{3})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leq C\left\lVert\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y;\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})}.

(125)

Proof.

For a function
\boldsymbol{u}\in H_{\#}^{1}(Y;\mathbb{C}^{3})
we have the Fourier series decomposition:

\boldsymbol{u}=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}a_{k}{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}k\cdot y},\quad\nabla\boldsymbol{u}=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}k\cdot y}a_{k}\otimes\left(2\pi{\rm i}k\right),

for which we can calculate the norms

\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y;\mathbb{C}^{3})}^{2}=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}|a_{k}|^{2},\quad\left\lVert\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y;\mathbb{C}^{3})}^{2}=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}|2\pi|^{2}|a_{k}\otimes k|^{2}.

Furthermore, we have

\nabla\boldsymbol{u}+\boldsymbol{u}\otimes{\rm i}\chi=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}k\cdot y}a_{k}\otimes\left(2\pi{\rm i}k+{\rm i}\chi\right),\quad\left\lVert\nabla\boldsymbol{u}+\boldsymbol{u}\otimes({\rm i}\chi)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y;\mathbb{C}^{3})}^{2}=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}|a_{k}\otimes\left(2\pi{\rm i}k+{\rm i}\chi\right)|^{2},

and therefore

\left\lVert(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi})\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y;\mathbb{C}^{3})}^{2}=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}|a_{k}\odot\left(2\pi{\rm i}k+{\rm i}\chi\right)|^{2}.

Combining this with the inequality
|\boldsymbol{a}\odot\boldsymbol{b}|\geq|\boldsymbol{a}||\boldsymbol{b}|/\sqrt{2},
we infer (
123
)–(
125
).
∎

The following is the result on the extension with dominated norm of symmetrized gradients:

Proposition 8.10
.

There exists an extension operator from
H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})
to
H_{\#}^{1}(Y;\mathbb{C}^{3})
such that (
\boldsymbol{u}\mapsto\boldsymbol{u}
):

\displaystyle\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(Y;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})},

(126)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y;\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})}\leq C\left\lVert\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})},

for all
\chi\in Y
, where the constant
C
depends only on
Y_{\rm stiff}
.

Proof.

This is a clear consequence of an analogous result for the extension operator applied to quasiperiodic functions. For the construction of the extension operator see
[
31
]
. ∎

We can now state the following two propositions.

Proposition 8.11
.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that for every
\boldsymbol{u}\in H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})
,
\chi\in Y^{\prime}\setminus\{0\}
we have the following estimate:

\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq\frac{C}{|\chi|}\left\lVert\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})}.

(127)

Proof.

By combining (
123
) and (
126
) we obtain

\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(Y;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq\frac{C}{|\chi|}\left\lVert\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y;\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})}\leq\frac{C}{|\chi|}\left\lVert\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})}.

∎

Proposition 8.12
.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that for all
\boldsymbol{u}\in H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})
,
\chi\in Y^{\prime}
one has

\displaystyle\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}-\fint_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C\left\lVert\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})},

(128)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}-\fint_{\Gamma}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}H^{1/2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C\left\lVert\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})}.

(129)

Proof.

The bound (
128
) is deduced from (
125
) and Proposition
8.10
similar to how Proposition
8.11
was established. To prove (
129
), note first that by (
128
) and the continuity of traces, one has

\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}-\fint_{\Gamma}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}-\fint_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}-\fint_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C\left\lVert\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})}.

(130)

Second, from Proposition
8.8
and the trace inequality, one has

\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}-\fint_{\Gamma}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C\left(\left\lVert\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\left(\boldsymbol{u}-\fint_{\Gamma}\boldsymbol{u}\right)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3\times 3})}+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}-\fint_{\Gamma}\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\right).

(131)

The bound (
129
) now follows from (
131
) by using (
130
), (
127
), and the continuity of traces.
∎

8.3
Well posedness and regularity of boundary value problems in elasticity

Here we state some results regarding the properties of the weak solution to the boundary value problem

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-\div\left(\mathbb{A}\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\right)=\boldsymbol{f}&\mbox{ on $\Omega$},\\[2.5pt]
(\mathbb{A}\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{u})\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}|_{\partial\Omega}+\boldsymbol{u}=\boldsymbol{g}&\mbox{ on $\partial\Omega$},\end{array}\right.

(132)

where
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
is a bounded domain,
\boldsymbol{f}\in L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})
,
\boldsymbol{g}\in H^{-1/2}(\partial\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})
and the tensor of material properties
\mathbb{A}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3\times 3\times 3})
satisfies the following assumption.

Assumption 8.1
.

•

Uniformly positive definiteness on symmetric matrices: there exists
\nu>0
such that

\nu|\xi|^{2}\leq\mathbb{A}(x)\xi:\xi\leq\nu^{-1}|\xi|^{2}
for all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3},\xi^{\top}=\xi,

x\in\Omega.

•

Material symmetries:

[\mathbb{A}]_{ijkl}=[\mathbb{A}]_{jikl}=[\mathbb{A}]_{klij},

i,j,k,l\in\{1,2,3\}.

The weak form of this problem is stated in the following definition:

Definition 8.1

(Robin boundary problem for elasticity, weak form)
.

For given functions
\boldsymbol{f}\in L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})
,
\boldsymbol{g}\in H^{-1/2}(\partial\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})
find
\boldsymbol{u}\in H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})
such that

\int_{\Omega}\mathbb{A}(x)\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{u}(x):\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(x)+\int_{\partial\Omega}\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\boldsymbol{v}=\int_{\Omega}\boldsymbol{f}\cdot\boldsymbol{v}+\int_{\partial\Omega}\boldsymbol{g}\cdot\boldsymbol{v}\qquad\forall\boldsymbol{v}\in H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3}).

Notice that the map
\boldsymbol{v}\mapsto\int_{\partial\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{g}\cdot\boldsymbol{v}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
is a bounded linear functional on
H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Also, the form

(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\mapsto\int_{\Omega}\mathbb{A}(x)\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{u}(x):\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(x)+\int_{\partial\Omega}\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\boldsymbol{v},

is coercive on
H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})
(by the Proposition
8.5
). Thus by Lax-Milgram, there exists a unique weak solution
\boldsymbol{u}\in H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})
of (
132
) in the sense of Definition
8.1
and we have

\lVert\boldsymbol{u}\rVert_{H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq C\left(\lVert\boldsymbol{f}\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}+\lVert\boldsymbol{g}\rVert_{H^{-1/2}(\partial\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}\right)^{1/2}.

The following result can be found in, e.g.
[
27
]
.

Lemma 8.13

(Regularity of the solution of Robin problem)
.

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
be a bounded domain with boundary
\partial\Omega
of class
C^{1,1}
. Let
\boldsymbol{f}\in L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})
,
\boldsymbol{g}\in H^{1/2}(\partial\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})
and, in addition to
8.1
,
\mathbb{A}\in\mathcal{C}^{0,1}(\overline{\Omega};\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3\times 3\times 3\times 3})
. Then the unique weak solution to the problem (
132
)
belongs to
H^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})
and we have

\lVert\boldsymbol{u}\rVert_{H^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq C\left(\lVert\boldsymbol{f}\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}+\lVert\boldsymbol{g}\rVert_{H^{1/2}(\partial\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}\right)^{1/2}.

We are also interested in the solution to the Dirichlet type boundary value problem:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-\div\left(\mathbb{A}\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\right)=\boldsymbol{f}&\mbox{on $\Omega$},\\[1.49994pt]
\boldsymbol{u}=\boldsymbol{g}&\mbox{ on $\partial\Omega$},\end{array}\right.

(133)

where
\boldsymbol{f}\in H^{-1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})
,
\boldsymbol{g}\in H^{1/2}(\partial\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})
. It is straight forward to apply theorem of Lax-Milgram to conclude that there exists unique weak solution
\boldsymbol{u}\in H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})
.
Again the proof of the following result can be found in
[
27
]
and is the standard regularity result for Dirichlet boundary problem.

Lemma 8.14

(Regularity of the solution of Dirichlet problem)
.

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
be a bounded domain with boundary
\partial\Omega
of class
C^{1,1}
. Let
\boldsymbol{f}\in L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})
,
\boldsymbol{g}\in H^{3/2}(\partial\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})
and
\mathbb{A}\in\mathcal{C}^{0,1}(\overline{\Omega};\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3\times 3\times 3\times 3})
. Then the unique weak solution to the problem (
133
)
belongs to
H^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})
and we have

\lVert\boldsymbol{u}\rVert_{H^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq C\left(\lVert\boldsymbol{f}\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}+\lVert\boldsymbol{g}\rVert_{H^{3/2}(\partial\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}\right)^{1/2}.

The constant
C
depends on
\Omega
and
\mathcal{C}^{0,1}
norm of
\mathbb{A}
.

8.3.1
Trace extension lemma

A version of the following theorem can be found in
[
5
]
.

Theorem 8.15
.

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
be a bounded domain with boundary
\partial\Omega
of class
C^{0,1}
. Let
\boldsymbol{g}_{0}\in H^{1}(\partial\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})
,
\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\in L^{2}(\partial\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Then, there exists
\boldsymbol{u}\in H^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})
such that

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\partial_{\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}}\boldsymbol{u}=\boldsymbol{g}_{1}&\quad\mbox{ on $\partial\Omega,$ }\\[2.5pt]
\boldsymbol{u}=\boldsymbol{g}_{0}&\quad\mbox{ on $\partial\Omega$}\end{array}\right.

if and only if

\nabla_{\partial\Omega}\boldsymbol{g}_{0}+\boldsymbol{g}_{1}\otimes\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}\in H^{1/2}(\partial\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3}).

(134)

We will prove the following lemma.

Lemma 8.16
.

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
be a bounded domain with boundary
\partial\Omega
of class
C^{1,1}
and
\mathbb{A}\in\mathcal{C}^{0,1}(\overline{\Omega};\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3\times 3\times 3})
. Let
\boldsymbol{g}\in H^{1/2}(\partial\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Then there exists
\boldsymbol{u}\in H^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})
such that

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathbb{A}\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}=\boldsymbol{g}&\quad\mbox{ on $\partial\Omega$, }\\[2.5pt]
\boldsymbol{u}=0&\quad\mbox{ on $\partial\Omega$. }\end{array}\right.

(135)

Proof.

The first step is to conclude the following: if a function
\boldsymbol{u}\in H^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})
satisfies
\boldsymbol{u}=0
on
\Gamma
then

\nabla\boldsymbol{u}|_{\partial\Omega}=\partial_{\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}}\boldsymbol{u}|_{\partial\Omega}\otimes\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}.

(136)

This can be seen by considering arbitrary vector
\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}=\alpha_{1}\vec{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{1}+\alpha_{2}\vec{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{2}+\alpha_{3}\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}
at any point in
\partial\Omega
, where
\vec{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{1},\vec{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{2},\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}
consists an orthonormal basis, vectors
\vec{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{1},\vec{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{2}
being tangential on
\partial\Omega
and
\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}
being normal to
\partial\Omega
. Now,

\nabla\boldsymbol{u}|_{\partial\Omega}\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}=\alpha_{1}\partial_{\vec{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{1}}\boldsymbol{u}|_{\partial\Omega}+\alpha_{2}\partial_{\vec{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{2}}\boldsymbol{u}|_{\partial\Omega}+\alpha_{3}\partial_{\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}}\boldsymbol{u}|_{\partial\Omega}=\alpha_{3}\partial_{\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}}\boldsymbol{u}|_{\partial\Omega},

due to the fact that
\partial_{\vec{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{1}}\boldsymbol{u}|_{\partial\Omega}=\partial_{\vec{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{2}}\boldsymbol{u}=0|_{\partial\Omega}
. But, since
\alpha_{3}=\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}
, one has

\nabla\boldsymbol{u}|_{\partial\Omega}\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}=\left(\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}\right)\partial_{\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}}\boldsymbol{u}|_{\partial\Omega}=\left(\partial_{\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}}\boldsymbol{u}|_{\partial\Omega}\otimes\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}\right)\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{v}}.

From this we have (
136
). With this at hand, the first equation in (
135
) becomes

\mathbb{A}\left(\partial_{\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}}\boldsymbol{u}|_{\partial\Omega}\odot\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}\right)\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}=\boldsymbol{g}.

We proceed by introducing the new variable
\boldsymbol{\omega}:=\partial_{\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}}\boldsymbol{u}|_{\partial\Omega}
and we consider the problem of finding
\boldsymbol{\omega}
such that

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{A}\left(\boldsymbol{\omega}\odot\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}\right)\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}=\boldsymbol{g}.

(137)

The second step of the proof is to conclude the following: There exists a unique solution
\boldsymbol{\omega}\in H^{1/2}(\partial\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})
to the problem (
137
).
Notice that (
137
) is an algebraic equation for every
x\in\partial\Omega
. For a fixed point
x\in\partial\Omega
, we introduce the operator
L_{x}:\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}^{3}
with the expression

L_{x}\boldsymbol{\omega}:=\mathbb{A}\left(\boldsymbol{\omega}\odot\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}(x)\right)\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{n}(x)}.

To see that it is symmetric and positive definite we perform the following calculations:

\displaystyle\langle L_{x}\boldsymbol{\omega},\boldsymbol{w}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbb{A}\left(\boldsymbol{\omega}\odot\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}(x)\right)\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}(x),\boldsymbol{w}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}=\mathbb{A}\left(\boldsymbol{\omega}\odot\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}(x)\right):\left(\boldsymbol{w}\odot\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}(x)\right)=\langle L_{x}\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{\omega}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}},

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\langle L_{x}\boldsymbol{\omega},\boldsymbol{\omega}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{A}\left(\boldsymbol{\omega}\odot\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}(x)\right):\left(\boldsymbol{\omega}\odot\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}(x)\right)\geq\nu\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|\boldsymbol{\omega}\odot\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}(x)|^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\geq\frac{\nu}{2}|\boldsymbol{\omega}|^{2}|\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}(x)|^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{\nu}{2}|\boldsymbol{\omega}|^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},

(138)

where we have used the Assumption
8.1
.
Thus, the problem

L_{x}\boldsymbol{\omega}(x)=\boldsymbol{g}(x)

has a unique solution for a.e.
x\in\partial\Omega
.
Clearly, from (
138
) it follows that
\det\left(L_{x}\right)\geq\alpha>0
, uniformly in
x\in\partial\Omega
. By
C^{0,1}
regularity of both the material coefficients and the normal
\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}
, we know that both
\det\left(L_{x}\right)
and
\left(\det\left(L_{x}\right)\right)^{-1}
are of class
C^{0,1}
. Then, by the application of Cramer’s rule, we arrive at the conclusion that the function
\boldsymbol{\omega}(x):=\left(L_{x}\right)^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}(x)
belongs to
H^{1/2}(\partial\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})
.
Thus, we have reduced the problem (
135
) to finding
\boldsymbol{u}\in H^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3})
such that

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\partial_{\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}}\boldsymbol{u}=\boldsymbol{\omega}&\quad\mbox{ on $\partial\Omega,$ }\\[2.5pt]
\boldsymbol{u}=0&\quad\mbox{ on $\partial\Omega$, }\end{array}\right.

where
\boldsymbol{\omega}
is a solution of (
137
).In order to complete the proof we only need to check the validity of the condition (
134
) and apply Theorem
8.15
, which can be applied since
\boldsymbol{\omega}\otimes\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}\in H^{1/2}(\partial\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{3}),

due to the
C^{0,1}(\partial\Omega;\mathbb{R})
regularity of the normal
\vec{\boldsymbol{n}}
.
∎

8.4
Auxiliary proofs

Proof of Theorem
4.3
.

The definition of
\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}
can be restated as follows:

\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{g}:=\boldsymbol{G}+\boldsymbol{u},\quad\boldsymbol{u}\in H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3}),

where

\displaystyle a_{0,\chi}^{\rm stiff}(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})=-a_{0,\chi}^{\rm stiff}(\boldsymbol{G},\boldsymbol{v})

\displaystyle\forall\boldsymbol{v}\in H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3}),\,\boldsymbol{v}|_{\Gamma}=0,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}|_{\Gamma}=0,

and
\boldsymbol{G}\in H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})
is an extension of
\boldsymbol{g}\in H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})
satisfying the bound

\left\lVert\boldsymbol{G}\right\rVert_{H^{1}}\leq C\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}\right\rVert_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}.

Recall that the form
a_{0,\chi}^{\rm stiff}:H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})\times H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})\to\mathbb{R}
is the sesquilinear form associated with the operator
\mathcal{A}_{0,\chi}^{\rm stiff}
.

We aim to find an expansion of the solution
\boldsymbol{u}\in H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})
,
\boldsymbol{u}|_{\Gamma}=0,
to

\displaystyle\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\boldsymbol{u}(y):\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy

(139)

\displaystyle=-\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\boldsymbol{G}(y):\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy\qquad\forall\boldsymbol{v}\in H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3}),\ \boldsymbol{v}|_{\Gamma}=0.

in the form

\boldsymbol{u}=\boldsymbol{u}_{0}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{u}_{n},

(140)

such that the
H^{1}
norm of each term
\boldsymbol{u}_{n}
is bounded with a bound of order
|\chi|^{n}
, while the error of approximation

\boldsymbol{u}_{\rm error,n}:=\boldsymbol{u}-\boldsymbol{u}_{0}-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{u}_{k}

is of order
|\chi|^{n+1}
. We assume that all terms of the series satisfy zero boundary condition on
\Gamma
. The leading order term
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}
corresponds to the harmonic lift in the case
\chi=0
.
With this apriori intention, we plug the expansion (
140
) into (
139
) and equate the terms which would be of order
\mathcal{O}(1)
to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{0}(y):\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy

\displaystyle=-\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{G}(y):\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy,

\displaystyle\forall\boldsymbol{v}\in H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3}),\quad\boldsymbol{v}|_{\Gamma}=0.

We recognise here the definition of the zeroth order lift operator
\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}
, namely

\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{g}=\boldsymbol{u}_{0}+\boldsymbol{G}=:\widetilde{\boldsymbol{u}}.

It is clear that

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C\left\lVert\boldsymbol{G}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}\right\rVert_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Next, with this term in hand, we proceed to define
\boldsymbol{u}_{1}
with:

\displaystyle\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{1}(y)

\displaystyle:\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy

(141)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{u}_{0}(y):\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy-\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{0}(y):{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy

\displaystyle-\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{G}(y):\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy-\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\sym\nabla G(y):{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}{\rm i}X_{\chi}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{u}}(y):\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy-\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\sym\nabla\widetilde{\boldsymbol{u}}(y):{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy,

\displaystyle\forall\boldsymbol{v}\in H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3}),\ \boldsymbol{v}|_{\Gamma}=0.

Clearly, one has

\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C|\chi|\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}\right\rVert_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}.
Proceeding next with terms of second order, we define next term in the expansion by

\displaystyle\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{2}(y)

\displaystyle:\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{u}_{0}(y):{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy-\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}{\rm i}X_{\chi}G(y):{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy

\displaystyle-\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{u}_{1}(y):\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy-\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{1}(y):{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}{\rm i}X_{\chi}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{u}}(y):{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy-\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{u}_{1}(y):\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy

\displaystyle-\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{1}(y):{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy\qquad\forall v\in H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3}),\ \boldsymbol{v}|_{\Gamma}=0.

We see that

\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}_{2}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C|\chi|^{2}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}\right\rVert_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}.
Continuing this process, we have

\displaystyle\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{3}(y)

\displaystyle:\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy=-\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{u}_{1}(y):{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy

\displaystyle-\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{u}_{2}(y):\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy-\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{2}(y):{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy

\displaystyle\forall\boldsymbol{v}\in H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3}),\ \boldsymbol{v}|_{\Gamma}=0,

with the bound

\lVert\boldsymbol{u}_{2}\rVert_{H^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C|\chi|^{3}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}\right\rVert_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}.
In general, by induction for
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we can define the term
\boldsymbol{u}_{n}
by the recurrence relation

\displaystyle\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(y)

\displaystyle:\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy=-\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{u}_{n-2}(y):{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy

\displaystyle-\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{u}_{n-1}(y):\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy-\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n-1}(y):{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy

\displaystyle\forall v\in H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3}),\ v|_{\Gamma}=0,

for which the defined sequence
\boldsymbol{u}_{n}
satisfies the bound

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C|\chi|^{n}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}\right\rVert_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}\qquad\forall n\in\mathbb{N}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

In this way, we can define the operators
\widetilde{\Pi}_{\chi,n}:\boldsymbol{h}\mapsto\boldsymbol{u}_{n}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}.

The error term

\boldsymbol{u}_{\rm error,n}:=\boldsymbol{u}-\sum_{k=0}^{n}\boldsymbol{u}_{k},\quad n\in\mathbb{N},

satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}_{\rm error,n}(y):\left(\sym\nabla+{\rm i}X_{\chi}\right)\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy

\displaystyle=-\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(y):{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy-\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{u}_{n-1}(y):{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy

\displaystyle-\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(y):\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy-\int_{Y_{\rm stiff}}\mathbb{A}_{\rm stiff}\sym\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(y):{\rm i}X_{\chi}\boldsymbol{v}(y)dy

\displaystyle\forall\boldsymbol{v}\in H_{\#}^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3}),\ \boldsymbol{v}|_{\Gamma}=0.

It follows that

\lVert\boldsymbol{u}_{\rm error,n}\rVert_{H^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C|\chi|^{n+1}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{g}\right\rVert_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})}.
∎

Proof of Lemma
6.1
.

The proof goes by representing the action of the projections
P_{\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}}
in the appropriate orthonormal basis. First, we denote with
\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{2},\boldsymbol{e}_{3}
an orthonormal basis of constant functions for
\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{0}
. Due to (
67
) we have:

\widehat{P}_{\chi}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}=\widehat{P}_{0}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}+\mathcal{O}(|\chi|)=\boldsymbol{e}_{i}+\mathcal{O}(|\chi|),\quad i=1,2,3,

where
O(|\chi|)
is understood in the sense of
H^{1/2}(\Gamma;\mathbb{C}^{3})
norm. Here we have used the fact that
\widehat{P}_{0}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}=e_{i}
.
Furthermore, due to (
68
), one has

\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\widehat{P}_{\chi}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}=\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\widehat{P}_{0}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}+\mathcal{O}(|\chi|)=\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}+\mathcal{O}(|\chi|)=\boldsymbol{e}_{j}+\mathcal{O}(|\chi|),\quad j=1,2,3,

(142)

where
O(|\chi|)
is understood in the sense of
H^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})
norm and we interpret
\Pi_{0}^{\rm stiff}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}
as in Remark
4.2
. Notice that

\left\{\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{1}}{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{e}_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}},\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{2}}{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{e}_{2}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}},\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{3}}{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{e}_{3}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}}\right\}

is an orthonormal basis for
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{0}^{\rm stiff}
. Now we define an orthonormal basis
\{\boldsymbol{e}_{1}^{\chi},\boldsymbol{e}_{2}^{\chi},\boldsymbol{e}_{3}^{\chi}\}
for
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}
by using the Gramm-Schmidt procedure:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{e}_{1}^{\chi}:=\frac{\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\widehat{P}_{\chi}\boldsymbol{e}_{1}}{\left\lVert\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\widehat{P}_{\chi}\boldsymbol{e}_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}},

(143)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{e}_{2}^{\chi}:=\frac{\boldsymbol{b}_{2}^{\chi}}{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{b}_{2}^{\chi}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}},\quad\boldsymbol{b}_{2}^{\chi}:=\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\widehat{P}_{\chi}\boldsymbol{e}_{2}-\left\langle\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\widehat{P}_{\chi}\boldsymbol{e}_{2},\boldsymbol{e}_{1}^{\chi}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}\boldsymbol{e}_{1}^{\chi},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{e}_{3}^{\chi}:=\frac{\boldsymbol{b}_{3}^{\chi}}{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{b}_{3}^{\chi}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}},\quad\boldsymbol{b}_{3}^{\chi}:=\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\widehat{P}_{\chi}\boldsymbol{e}_{3}-\left\langle\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\widehat{P}_{\chi}\boldsymbol{e}_{3},\boldsymbol{e}_{1}^{\chi}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}\boldsymbol{e}_{1}^{\chi}-\left\langle\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\widehat{P}_{\chi}\boldsymbol{e}_{3},\boldsymbol{e}_{2}^{\chi}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}\boldsymbol{e}_{2}^{\chi}.

From (
142
) we also infer that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\widehat{P}_{\chi}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}+\mathcal{O}(|\chi|),\quad i=1,2,3,

\displaystyle\bigl\langle\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\widehat{P}_{\chi}\boldsymbol{e}_{i},\Pi_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}\widehat{P}_{\chi}\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\bigr\rangle_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}_{i},\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}+\mathcal{O}(|\chi|)=\mathcal{O}(|\chi|),\quad i\neq j.

(144)

It follows from (
143
)–(
144
) that

\|\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\chi}-\boldsymbol{e}_{i}\|_{H^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C|\chi|.

Therefore, for
\boldsymbol{f}\in L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3}),
one has

P_{\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}^{\rm stiff}}\boldsymbol{f}=\sum_{i=1}^{3}\left\langle\boldsymbol{f},\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\chi}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}\boldsymbol{e}_{i}^{\chi}=\sum_{i=1}^{3}\left\langle\boldsymbol{f},\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{1}}{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{e}_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{1}}{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{e}_{1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}}+\mathcal{R}_{\rm error}^{\chi},

where

\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\rm error}^{\chi}\rVert_{H^{1}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}\leq C\left\lVert\boldsymbol{f}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(Y_{\rm stiff};\mathbb{C}^{3})}|\chi|.

∎
